Twisted restricted conformal blocks of vertex operator algebras I: g g -twisted correlation functions and fusion rules
Xu Gao, Jianqi Liu, Yiyi Zhu

Abstract
In this paper, we introduce a notion of g g -twisted restricted conformal block on the three-pointed twisted projective line 𝔵 : C ¯ → P 1 \mathfrak{x}\colon\overline{C}\to\mathbb{P}^{1} associated with an untwisted module M 1 M^{1} and the bottom levels of two g g -twisted modules M 2 M^{2} and M 3 M^{3} over a vertex operator algebra V V . We show that the space of twisted restricted conformal blocks is isomorphic to the space of g g -twisted (restricted) correlation functions defined by the same datum and to the space of intertwining operators among these twisted modules. As an application, we derive a twisted version of the Fusion Rules Theorem.



MSC Classification]17B69, 81T40

keywords
vertex operator algebra, fusion rule, twisted module, restricted conformal block

1
Introduction

This is the first paper in a series aiming to explore the general theory of twisted (restricted) conformal blocks of vertex operator algebras. In this paper, we focus on their
g
-twisted correlation functions and the fusion rules among
g
-twisted modules.

The concept of
twisted
representations of
vertex operator algebras
(VOAs for short) originated in the realization of irreducible representations of twisted affine Lie algebras
Lepowsky 1985
, the construction of the celebrated moonshine module
Frenkel et al. 1988
, and the study of orbifold models in conformal field theory
Dixon et al. 1985
;
Dijkgraaf et al. 1989
.
Over the past few decades, twisted representations have been extensively studied, e.g.
Dong and Lepowsky 1993
;
Xu 1995
;
Dong et al. 1998
;
Dong et al. 2000
;
Dong and Yamskulna 2002
;
Barron et al. 2002
.
One of the most notable applications is in the
orbifold theory
of VOAs
Dong et al. 2000
;
Dong and Yamskulna 2002
;
Carnahan and Miyamoto 2016
;
Dong et al. 2017
.
The renowned
orbifold conjecture
posits that every irreducible module over the fixed-point subVOA
V^{G}
of
V
under some finite automorphism group
G<\operatorname{Aut}(V)
occurs in an irreducible
g
-twisted
V
-module for some
g\in G
, and if
V
is strongly rational, then
V^{G}
also follows suit.
Recent breakthroughs have established the validity of this conjecture for cyclic groups
Miyamoto 2015
;
Carnahan and Miyamoto 2016
;
Dong et al. 2017
.
This has led to numerous new examples of strongly rational VOAs with irreducible modules emerging as direct summands of certain twisted modules.

In the landscape of VOA theory and the associated
conformal field theory
(CFT for short), a crucial challenge is ascertaining the fusion algebra within the module category, entailing the computation of fusion rules among irreducible modules.
By definition, the
fusion rule
associated to
V
-modules
M^{1}
,
M^{2}
, and
M^{3}
is the dimension of the space of
intertwining operators
among them.
In the context of certain rational orbifold CFTs, the application of the renowned
Verlinde Formula

Verlinde 1988
has allowed the determination of fusion rules through a concrete description of the
S
-matrix in their modular transformations
Dijkgraaf et al. 1989
.
On the VOA side, due to the intricate nature of twisted irreducible representations, the fusion rules were only established for certain
\mathbb Z/2\mathbb Z
or
\mathbb Z/3\mathbb Z
-orbifold lattice VOAs.
For instance, in the case of the
\theta
-cyclic orbifold VOAs
M(1)^{+}
and
V_{L}^{+}
introduced in
Frenkel et al. 1988
, fusion rules were determined in
Abe et al. 2005
through explicit constructions of twisted intertwining operators for Heisenberg and lattice VOAs.
In general, when dealing with an arbitrary strongly rational VOA
V
, the connection between fusion rules among ordinary modules over the orbifold VOA
V^{G}
and fusion rules among twisted modules over
V
remains elusive, and a unified method for computing fusion rules among twisted modules is currently lacking.

Let
V
be a VOA, and let
g_{1},g_{2}
, and
g_{3}
be three finite-order automorphisms of
V
. The concept of
twisted intertwining operators
among
g_{1},g_{2},
and
g_{3}
-twisted
V
-modules
M^{1}
,
M^{2}
, and
M^{3}
was initially introduced by Xu in
Xu 1995
.
Xu’s definition generalizes the usual
Jacobi identity
of untwisted intertwining operators in
Frenkel et al. 1993
by incorporating factors involving rational powers of formal variables.
In addition, Huang has further extended the notion of twisted modules and twisted intertwining operators to arbitrary (not necessarily commuting or of finite order) automorphisms
g_{1},g_{2},
and
g_{3}
in
Huang 2010
;
Huang 2018
by generalizing the duality properties of untwisted intertwining operators.

One approach to fusion rules from the geometric side is by exploring
conformal blocks
on algebraic curves associated to modules/sectors.
Notably, the isomorphism between the space of
correlation functions
of conformal blocks on the three-pointed complex projective line
(\mathbb{P}^{1},\infty,1,0)
associated to irreducible
V
-modules
M^{2}
,
(M^{3})^{\prime}
, and
M^{1}
, and the space of intertwining operators of type
\textstyle\binom{M^{3}}{M^{1}\;M^{2}}
is well-known, as established in
Tsuchiya et al. 1989
;
Moore and Seiberg 1989
;
Zhu 1994
;
Liu 2023
.
Furthermore, conformal blocks can be reconstructed from their restrictions on the
bottom levels

M^{2}(0)
and
M^{3}(0)^{\ast}
, as established in
Zhu 1996
;
Liu 2023
.
Then, in this context, the fusion rule
N\textstyle\binom{M^{3}}{M^{1}\;M^{2}}
can be computed through the modules
M^{2}(0)
and
M^{3}(0)^{\ast}
, and the bimodule
A(M^{1})
over
Zhu’s algebra

A(V)
for the VOA
V
.
This is the well-known
Fusion Rules Theorem
claimed in
Frenkel and Zhu 1992
.
While the concept of conformal block for twisted modules has been formulated by Frenkel and Szczesny in
Frenkel and Ben-Zvi 2004
;
Frenkel and Szczesny 2004
, the twisted version of the aforementioned story remains unexplored.

In the present work, we address these questions in the simplest nontrivial scenario where
g_{1}=1
and
g_{2}=g_{3}
, namely, when the
V
-module
M^{1}
is untwisted, while
M^{2}
and
M^{3}
are both
g
-twisted for some automorphism
g
of order
T<\infty
. We will refer to this scenario as the
g
-twisted
case.
Let
I
be a twisted intertwining operator among
M^{1}
,
M^{2}
, and
M^{3}
.
In order to accommodate the rational powers
z^{1/T}
and
w^{1/T}
occurring in the twisted fields
Y_{M^{2}}(-,z)
,
Y_{M^{3}}(-,z)
, and
I(-,w)
simultaneously, we introduce the
T
-twisted projective line

\mathfrak{x}\colon\overline{C}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
.
On this curve, we attach
M^{2}
to
0
,
(M^{3})^{\prime}
to
\infty
, and
M^{1}
to a point
1\in\mathbb{P}^{1}
that is other than
0
and
\infty
.
In the spirit of
Tsuchiya et al. 1989
;
Zhu 1996
;
Liu 2023
, the space of
g
-twisted correlation functions associated to the datum

(\mathfrak{x}\colon\overline{C}\to\mathbb{P}^{1},\infty,1,0,(M^{3})^{\prime},M^{1},M^{2})
can be defined by axiomizing the behaviors of the limit function (on
\overline{C}
) of the
Puiseux
series

\left\langle v^{\prime}_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}Y_{M^{3}}(a^{1},z_{1})\cdots Y_{M^{3}}(a^{k-1},z_{k-1})I(v,w)Y_{M^{2}}(a^{k},z_{k})\cdots Y_{M^{2}}(a^{n},z_{n})v_{2}\right\rangle w^{h},

(1.1)

where
v^{\prime}_{3}\in(M^{3})^{\prime}
,
v_{2}\in M^{2}
,
v\in M^{1}
, and
a^{i}\in V
.
Denote this space by
\operatorname{Cor}\textstyle\binom{M^{3}}{M^{1}\;M^{2}}
.
For the generality, we introduce a space
\operatorname{Cor}\left(\Sigma_{1}(N^{3},M^{1},M^{2})\right)
of
g
-twisted correlation functions
associated to the datum

\Sigma_{1}(N^{3},M^{1},M^{2}):=(\mathfrak{x}\colon\overline{C}\to\mathbb{P}^{1},\infty,1,0,N^{3},M^{1},M^{2})
, where
N^{3}
is an arbitrary
g^{-1}
-twisted
V
-module. See
2.8
.
Our first main theorem (
Theorem

2.24
) establishes an isomorphism between
\operatorname{Cor}\textstyle\binom{M^{3}}{M^{1}\;M^{2}}
and the space
\mathfrak{I}\textstyle\binom{M^{3}}{M^{1}\;M^{2}}
of
g
-twisted intertwining operators
.
To extend our construction to the general case where
M^{1}
is also twisted, we have to introduce twisted curves of higher genus.
For instance, the case when
g_{3}=g_{1}g_{2}=g_{2}g_{1}
and
g_{1}^{T}=g_{2}^{T}=1
involves the
Fermat curve
of degree
T
, which has genus
\frac{(T-1)(T-2)}{2}
. This will be addressed in a subsequent work.

To extend the
Fusion Rules Theorem
to the
g
-twisted scenario, we introduce an auxiliary space
\operatorname{Cor}(\Sigma_{1}(U^{3},M^{1},U^{2}))
of
g
-twisted restricted correlation functions
associated to the datum
\Sigma_{1}(U^{3},M^{1},U^{2}):=(\mathfrak{x}\colon\overline{C}\to\mathbb{P}^{1},\infty,1,0,

U^{3},

M^{1},U^{2})
, where
U^{2}
(resp.
U^{3}
) is an irreducible left (resp. right) module over the
g
-twisted Zhu’s algebra

A_{g}(V)
introduced in
Dong et al. 1998
.
The axioms we impose on
\operatorname{Cor}(\Sigma_{1}(U^{3},M^{1},U^{2}))
are based on behaviors of the limit function of the Puiseux series
1.1
, where
u_{2}\in M^{2}(0)
and
u^{\prime}_{3}\in M^{3}(0)^{\ast}
.
A crucial difference between our axioms on
\operatorname{Cor}(\Sigma_{1}(U^{3},M^{1},U^{2}))
and those in
Liu 2023
is the additional non-integer shifting of the coefficient functions
F_{n,i}(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{q})
in the recursive formulas, due to the ramification of
\overline{C}
at the points
0
and
\infty
.
Our second main theorem (
Theorem

3.16
) establishes an isomorphism between the space of
g
-twisted restricted correlation functions
\operatorname{Cor}(\Sigma_{1}(U^{3},M^{1},U^{2}))
and the space of
g
-twisted correlation functions
\operatorname{Cor}\left(\Sigma_{1}(\overline{M}(U^{3}),M^{1},\overline{M}(U^{2}))\right)
, where
\overline{M}(U^{2})
is the
g
-twisted generalized Verma module
associated to
U^{2}
, and
\overline{M}(U^{3})
is the
g^{-1}
-twisted generalized Verma module
associated to the right
A_{g}(V)
-module
U^{3}

Dong et al. 1998
. In the untwisted scenario, it was pointed out by Li in
Li 1999
that the
Fusion Rules Theorem
does not hold for arbitrary
M^{2}
and
M^{3}
.

The axioms of
\operatorname{Cor}(\Sigma_{1}(U^{3},M^{1},U^{2}))
imply, in particular, that a system of correlation functions
S
defines a linear functional
\varphi_{S}\colon u_{3}\otimes v\otimes u_{2}\mapsto S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle w^{\deg v}\in\mathbb C
on the vector space
U^{3}\otimes M^{1}\otimes U^{2}
.
Furthermore, the linear functional
\varphi_{S}
vanishes on a subspace
J
whose definition will be provided in
Definition

4.1
. The vanishing of
\varphi_{S}
on
J
can be interpreted as being invariant under the actions of the twisted chiral Lie algebras constrained at
\infty
and
0
.
We call the space
(U^{3}\otimes M^{1}\otimes U^{2})/J
the space of
g
-twisted restricted coinvariants
, and the dual space
((U^{3}\otimes M^{1}\otimes U^{2})/J)^{\ast}
the space of
g
-twisted restricted conformal blocks
, denoted by
\operatorname{{C}}\left(U^{3},M^{1},U^{2}\right)
.
In
Section

4
and
Section

5
, we show that there is a one-to-one correspondence between the space of
g
-twisted restricted conformal blocks and the space of
g
-twisted restricted correlation functions (
Theorem

4.5
) by reconstructing a system of correlation functions from a given restricted conformal block
\varphi
using the recursive formulas.
In the twisted case, the occurrence of non-integer shifting of the coefficient functions
F_{n,i}(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{q})
poses several challenges. Our main theorem in
Section

4
and
Section

5
can also be viewed as the
g
-twisted and restricted version of the “propagation of vacua” theorem in
Tsuchiya et al. 1989
;
Zhu 1994
;
Frenkel and Ben-Zvi 2004
;
Nagatomo and Tsuchiya 2005
.

The following diagram summarizes our main theorems in
Section

2
–
Section

5
, where we assume that
M^{1}
is an untwisted
V
-module, and
M^{2}
and
M^{3}
are admissible
g
-twisted
V
-modules such that
M^{2}
and
(M^{3})^{\prime}
are generalized Verma modules, with bottom levels
M^{2}(0)=U^{2}
and
M^{3}(0)^{\ast}=U^{3}
being irreducible
A_{g}(V)
-modules:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Cor}\textstyle\binom{M^{3}}{M^{1}\;M^{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathfrak{I}\textstyle\binom{M^{3}}{M^{1}\;M^{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Cor}\left(\Sigma_{1}(U^{3},M^{1},U^{2})\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{{C}}\left(U^{3},M^{1},U^{2}\right)}
Theorem
2.24
Theorem
3.16
Theorem
4.5

In particular, when
V
is
g
-rational
Dong et al. 1998
, the space
\mathfrak{I}\textstyle\binom{M^{3}}{M^{1}\;M^{2}}
of intertwining operators is isomorphic to the space of
g
-twisted restricted conformal blocks
\operatorname{{C}}\left(U^{3},M^{1},U^{2}\right)
, for arbitrary irreducible
g
-twisted
V
-modules
M^{2}
and
M^{3}
. In a subsequent paper, we will introduce the notions of twisted conformal blocks
\operatorname{{C}}\left(\Sigma_{1}(N^{3},M^{1},M^{2})\right)
and twisted restricted conformal blocks
\operatorname{{C}}\left(\Sigma_{1}(U^{3},M^{1},U^{2})\right)
using the actions of (constrained) twisted chiral Lie algebra, and demonstrate the isomorphisms in the following diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Cor}\left(\Sigma_{1}(N^{3},M^{1},M^{2})\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{{C}}\left(\Sigma_{1}(N^{3},M^{1},M^{2})\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Cor}\left(\Sigma_{1}(U^{3},M^{1},U^{2})\right)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{{C}}\left(\Sigma_{1}(U^{3},M^{1},U^{2})\right).}

Furthermore, we will show in
Section

6
that the space of
g
-twisted restricted coinvariants
(U^{3}\otimes M^{1}\otimes U^{2})/J
is isomorphic to both
U^{3}\otimes_{A_{g}(V)}B_{g,h}(M^{1})\otimes_{A_{g}(V)}U^{2}
and
U^{3}\otimes_{A_{g}(V)}A_{g}(M^{1})\otimes_{A_{g}(V)}U^{2}
, where
B_{g,h}(M^{1})
is an
A_{g}(V)
-bimodule generalizing
B_{h}(M^{1})
in
Liu 2023
, and
A_{g}(M^{1})
is an
A_{g}(V)
-bimodule constructed in
Jiang and Jiao 2016
that generalizes
A(M^{1})
in
Frenkel and Zhu 1992
. Consequently, we have multiple methods to compute the fusion rules
N\textstyle\binom{M^{3}}{M^{1}\;M^{2}}
when
M^{2}
and
(M^{3})^{\prime}
are
g
-twisted generalized Verma modules.
Notably, the isomorphism

\mathfrak{I}\textstyle\binom{M^{3}}{M^{1}\;M^{2}}\cong(M^{3}(0)^{\ast}\otimes_{A_{g}(V)}A_{g}(M^{1})\otimes_{A_{g}(V)}M^{2}(0))^{\ast}

extends the renowned
(untwisted) Fusion Rules Theorem
in
Frenkel and Zhu 1992
;
Li 1999
;
Huang and Yang 2012
;
Liu 2023
to the
g
-twisted case.
We also deduce several applications of the
g
-twisted Fusion Rules Theorem
. First, we establish the finiteness of
g
-twisted fusion rules under the assumption that
V
is
C_{2}
-cofinite. Secondly, when
V
is strongly rational, using the main theorem of
Carnahan and Miyamoto 2016
, we find the relation between
g
-twisted fusion rules among irreducible
g
-twisted
V
-modules and the ordinary fusion rules among irreducible
V^{0}
-modules by decomposing
M^{1}
,
M^{2}
, and
M^{3}
into direct sums of irreducible modules over
V^{0}
.

Lastly, in
Section

7
, we determine the fusion rules among irreducible
\theta
-twisted modules over the
Heisenberg VOA

M(1)
and rank one
lattice VOA

V_{L}
with
L=\mathbb Z\alpha
and
(\alpha|\alpha)=2
. This is achieved through the calculation of
A_{\theta}(M(1,\lambda))
and
A_{\theta}(V_{L+\frac{1}{2}\alpha})
, where
\theta
is the standard involution of
M(1)
and
V_{L}

Frenkel et al. 1988
. In these examples, the
\theta
-twisted fusion rules encompass all possibilities of fusion rules among
\theta
-twisted modules, given that
\theta^{2}=1
.

This paper is structured as follows. In
Section

2
, we introduce the
twisted projective line

\mathfrak{x}\colon\overline{C}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
and the space
\operatorname{Cor}\left(\Sigma_{1}(N^{3},M^{1},M^{2})\right)
of
g
-twsited correlation functions
. The key result in this section establishes the isomorphism between
\operatorname{Cor}\textstyle\binom{M^{3}}{M^{1}\;M^{2}}
and
\mathfrak{I}\textstyle\binom{M^{3}}{M^{1}\;M^{2}}
.
In
Section

3
, we introduce the space
\operatorname{Cor}(\Sigma_{1}(U^{3},M^{1},U^{2}))
of
g
-twisted restricted correlation functions
and demonstrate its isomorphism to
\operatorname{Cor}\left(\Sigma_{1}(\overline{M}(U^{3}),M^{1},\overline{M}(U^{2}))\right)
.
In
Section

4
, we reconstruct a system of correlation functions
S_{\varphi}
from a
g
-twisted restricted conformal block
\varphi
in
\operatorname{{C}}\left(U^{3},M^{1},U^{2}\right)
and establish the locality of
S_{\varphi}
. In
Section

5
, we demonstrate the associativity and other axioms of the reconstructed
S_{\varphi}
. In
Section

6
, we prove the
g
-twisted fusion rules theorem
and discuss its applications. Finally, in
Section

7
, we compute the fusion rules among
\theta
-twisted modules over the Heisenberg VOAs and the rank one lattice VOA using the
g
-twisted fusion rules theorem
.

Convention

In this paper, we adopt a specific formatting convention to enhance clarity. Text that we want to emphasize, terms with clear contextual meanings, or results available in standard textbooks will be presented in
italic font
. Whereas terminology introduced in the context will be in
bold font
.

We adhere to the following mathematical notation:
\mathbb N
denotes the set of natural numbers, including
0
;
\mathbb Z
stands for the ring of integers;
\mathbb Q
represents the field of rational numbers, and
\mathbb C
denotes the field of complex numbers.
All vector spaces are defined over
\mathbb C
.
Tensor products are over
\mathbb C
unless otherwise specified.

2
Space of twisted correlation functions

2.1
Preliminaries

Throughout this article, we fix a VOA
(V,Y,\mathbb{1},\upomega)
and an automorphism
g\in\operatorname{Aut}(V)
of order
T
.

The VOA
V
is then decomposed into
g
-eigenspaces

V^{r}=\left\{a\in V\nonscript\>\middle|\allowbreak\nonscript\>\mathopen{}g.a=e^{2\pi\mathbb{i}\frac{r}{T}}a\right\}.

Notably,
V^{0}
forms a subVOA of
V
, and each
V^{r}
serves as a module over
V^{0}
, utilizing the same vertex operator
Y
(cf.
Dong and Lepowsky 1993
;
Dong et al. 1998
;
Frenkel et al. 1988
).
Throughout this article, we keep the convention
0\leqslant r\leqslant T-1
for the superscript
r
.
Unless otherwise specified, when we talk about (weak, admissible, etc.)
module
, we mean (weak, admissible, etc.)
V
-modules
.
We will consistently use notations like
a({\textstyle n})
to denote the elements in the Lie algebra
\mathcal{L}_{g}(V)
(cf.
2.4
) and notations like
a_{(n)}
to denote the components of a vertex operator
Y(a,z)
or an intertwining operator
I(a,z)
.

Twisted modules and the twisted Jacobi identity

Recall the following definition:

Definition 2.1

(
Dong et al. 1998
)
.

A
weak
g
-twisted
V
-module
is a vector space
M
equipped with a linear map

Y_{M}\colon V\longrightarrow\operatorname{End}(M)\{z\},\qquad a\longmapsto Y_{M}(a,z)=\sum_{n\in\mathbb Q}a_{(n)}z^{-n-1},

satisfying the following axioms for all
a\in V^{r}
,
b\in V
, and
u\in M
:

•

Index property
:
Y_{M}(a,z)=\sum_{n\in\frac{r}{T}+\mathbb Z}a_{(n)}z^{-n-1}
.

•

Truncation property
:
a_{(n)}u=0
for
n\gg 0
.

•

Vacuum property
:
Y_{M}(\mathbb{1},z)=\operatorname{id}_{M}
.

•

Twisted Jacobi identity
:

\displaystyle z_{0}^{-1}\operatorname{\delta}\left(\dfrac{z_{1}-z_{2}}{z_{0}}\right)Y_{M}(a,z_{1})Y_{M}(b,z_{2})u-z_{0}^{-1}\operatorname{\delta}\left(\dfrac{-z_{2}+z_{1}}{z_{0}}\right)Y_{M}(b,z_{2})Y_{M}(a,z_{1})u

(2.1)

\displaystyle=z_{2}^{-1}\left(\dfrac{z_{1}-z_{0}}{z_{2}}\right)^{-\frac{r}{T}}\operatorname{\delta}\left(\dfrac{z_{1}-z_{0}}{z_{2}}\right)Y_{M}(Y(a,z_{0})b,z_{2})u.

A weak
g
-twisted
V
-module
M
is called an
admissible
g
-twisted
V
-module
if it admits a subspace decomposition
M=\bigoplus_{n\in\frac{1}{T}\mathbb N}M(n)
such that

a_{(m)}M(n)\subseteq M(\operatorname{wt}a-m-1+n)

(2.2)

for any homogeneous
a\in V
, any
m\in\mathbb Z
, and any
n\in\frac{1}{T}\mathbb N
.

A weak
g
-twisted
V
-module
M
is called a
g
-twisted
V
-module
if
L_{(0)}
acts on it semi-simply with finite dimensional eigenspaces
M_{\lambda}
, and the following property holds: for each
\lambda\in\mathbb C
, the eigenspace
M_{\lambda+\frac{n}{T}}
vanishes when
n\in\mathbb Z
is sufficiently small.

For a
formal Puiseux series

f(z)\in\mathbb C[\![{z^{\frac{1}{T}}}]\!]
, we employ the symbol
\operatorname{Res}_{z}f(z)
for the coefficient of
z^{-1}
in
f(z)
.
Multiplying the
twisted Jacobi identity

2.1
with
z^{m+\frac{r}{T}}_{1}z_{2}^{n+\frac{s}{T}}z_{0}^{l}
and then applying
\operatorname{Res}_{z_{0}}\operatorname{Res}_{z_{1}}\operatorname{Res}_{z_{2}}
, we obtain its component form as follows:

Lemma 2.2
.

Let
M
be a weak
g
-twisted module.
Then, for any
a\in V^{r}
,
b\in V^{s}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{i\geqslant 0}\binom{l}{i}(-1)^{i}a_{(\frac{r}{T}+m+l-i)}b_{(\frac{s}{T}+n+i)}-\sum_{i\geqslant 0}\binom{l}{i}(-1)^{l+i}b_{(\frac{s}{T}+n+l-i)}a_{(\frac{r}{T}+m+i)}

(2.3)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\geqslant 0}\binom{m+\frac{r}{T}}{j}(a_{(j+l)}b)_{(\frac{r+s}{T}+m+n-j)}

holds for all
m,n,l\in\mathbb Z
, where
a_{(j+l)}b:=\operatorname{Res}_{z}z^{j+l}Y(a,z)b
.

By
Lemma

2.2
and taking into account
(
Dong et al. 1998
, (3.5))
, we can readily establish a twisted version of the
duality property
:

Proposition 2.3
.

Let
(M,Y_{M})
be an admissible
g
-twisted module and
M^{\prime}
be its graded dual space.
Then, for any
a\in V^{r}
,
b\in V^{s}
, and any
u\in M
,
u^{\prime}\in M^{\prime}
, there exists a rational function
f(z_{1},z_{2})
with possible poles only at
z_{1}=0,z_{2}=0
, and
z_{1}=z_{2}
,
such that the following identities of formal Laurent
1
1

1

Note that there are no fractional powers involved.
series hold:

\displaystyle\left\langle u^{\prime}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}Y_{M}(a,z_{1})Y_{M}(b,z_{2})u\right\rangle z^{\frac{r}{T}}_{1}z^{\frac{s}{T}}_{2}

\displaystyle=\iota_{z_{1},z_{2}}f(z_{1},z_{2}),

\displaystyle\left\langle u^{\prime}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}Y_{M}(b,z_{2})Y_{M}(a,z_{1})u\right\rangle z^{\frac{r}{T}}_{1}z^{\frac{s}{T}}_{2}

\displaystyle=\iota_{z_{2},z_{1}}f(z_{1},z_{2}),

\displaystyle\left\langle u^{\prime}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}Y_{M}(Y(a,z_{1}-z_{2})b,z_{2})u\right\rangle(z_{2}+z_{1}-z_{2})^{\frac{r}{T}}z^{\frac{s}{T}}_{2}

\displaystyle=\iota_{z_{2},z_{1}-z_{2}}f(z_{1},z_{2}),

where
\iota_{z_{1},z_{2}},\iota_{z_{2},z_{1}}
, and
\iota_{z_{2},z_{1}-z_{2}}
send a rational function
f(z_{1},z_{2})
to its Laurent series expansions in the domains
|z_{1}|>|z_{2}|
,
|z_{2}|>|z_{1}|
, and
|z_{2}|>|z_{1}-z_{2}|
respectively.
Furthermore, the component form of the twisted Jacobi identity
2.3
is equivalent to the existence of such a rational function.

The associated Lie algebra and lowest-weight modules

Let’s recall the Lie algebra
\mathcal{L}_{g}(V)
associated to
a VOA
V
and an automorphism
g
of
V
with order
T
,
as introduced in
Dong et al. 1998
.
The automorphism
g
can be extended to the vertex algebra
V\otimes\mathbb C[t^{\pm\nicefrac{{1}}{{T}}}]
by

g(a\otimes t^{\frac{m}{T}}):=e^{-2\pi\mathbb{i}\frac{m}{T}}(ga\otimes t^{\frac{m}{T}}).

Denote the
g
-invariant subspace of
V\otimes\mathbb C[t^{\pm\nicefrac{{1}}{{T}}}]
by
\mathcal{L}(V,g)
.
It is clear that
\mathcal{L}(V,g)
is a sub-vertex algebra of
V\otimes\mathbb C[t^{\pm\nicefrac{{1}}{{T}}}]
, with the translation operator
\nabla:=L_{(-1)}\otimes\operatorname{id}+\operatorname{id}\otimes\frac{\mathinner{\partial}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}}{\mathinner{\partial}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{t}}
.
Then,
\mathcal{L}_{g}(V)
is the quotient

\mathcal{L}_{g}(V):=\mathcal{L}(V,g)/\nabla\mathcal{L}(V,g).

(2.4)

For any
m\in\mathbb Z
and
a\in V
, we denote the equivalent class of
a\otimes t^{\frac{m}{T}}
in
\mathcal{L}_{g}(V)
by
a({\textstyle\frac{m}{T}})
.
Then,
\mathcal{L}_{g}(V)
is a Lie algebra, with the Lie bracket given by

\left[a({\textstyle m+\frac{r}{T}}),b({\textstyle n+\frac{s}{T}})\right]=\sum_{j\geqslant 0}\binom{m+\frac{r}{T}}{j}(a_{(j)}b)({\textstyle m+n+\frac{r+s}{T}-j}),

for any
a\in V^{r},b\in V^{s}
, and
m,n\in\mathbb Z
.
Moreover,
\mathcal{L}_{g}(V)
has a natural gradation given by
\deg a({\textstyle\frac{m}{T}})=\operatorname{wt}a-\frac{m}{T}-1
,
where
m\in\mathbb Z
and
a
is a homogeneous element of
V
.
Let
\mathcal{L}_{g}(V)_{n}
be the subspace of
\mathcal{L}_{g}(V)
spanned by elements of degree
n\in\frac{1}{T}\mathbb Z
.
Then, we have a triangular decomposition:

\mathcal{L}_{g}(V)=\mathcal{L}_{g}(V)_{-}\oplus\mathcal{L}_{g}(V)_{0}\oplus\mathcal{L}_{g}(V)_{+},

where
\mathcal{L}_{g}(V)_{\pm}=\bigoplus_{n\in\frac{1}{T}\mathbb{Z}_{>0}}\mathcal{L}_{g}(V)_{\pm n}
.
Recall the following result in
Dong et al. 1998
;
Li 1999
:

Proposition 2.4
.

Let
M
be a weak
g
-twisted module. Then, the linear map

\mathcal{L}_{g}(V)\longrightarrow\operatorname{End}(M),\quad a({\textstyle\frac{m}{T}})\longmapsto\operatorname{Res}_{z}Y_{M}(a,z)z^{\frac{m}{T}}

defines a representation of the Lie algebra
\mathcal{L}_{g}(V)
on
M
.
Furthermore, if
M
is equipped with a
\frac{1}{T}\mathbb N
-gradation, then
M
is an admissible
g
-twisted module if and only if
M
is a graded module for the graded Lie algebra
\mathcal{L}_{g}(V)
.

Recall the following definition in
Li 1999
:

Definition 2.5
.

A weak
g
-twisted module
M
is called a
lowest-weight module
if there exists
h\in\mathbb C
such that the
L_{(0)}
-eigenspace
M_{h}
with eigenvalue
h
is an irreducible
\mathcal{L}_{g}(V)_{0}
-module, and
M={U}(\mathcal{L}_{g}(V)_{+})M_{h}
.

If this is the case, then
L_{(0)}
acts on
M
semi-simply with eigenvalues in
h+\frac{1}{T}\mathbb Z
.
We denote the eigenspace
M_{h+\frac{n}{T}}
by
M(\tfrac{n}{T})
, and write

\deg u=\frac{n}{T}
and
\operatorname{wt}u=h+\frac{n}{T}
for any homogeneous element
u\in M(\tfrac{n}{T})
.
Then,
M=\oplus_{n\in\mathbb N}M(\frac{n}{T})
is an admissible
g
-twisted module.

Definition 2.6
.

An admissible
g
-twisted module
M=\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbb N}M(\frac{n}{T})
is said to be
of conformal weight
h\in\mathbb C
, if
L_{(0)}
acts on
M
semi-simply and each eigenspace
M_{h+\frac{n}{T}}
is precisely
M(\frac{n}{T})
.

Definition 2.7
.

Let
M=\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbb{N}}M(\frac{n}{T})
be an admissible
g
-twisted module.
Then, its
graded dual space

M^{\prime}=\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbb{N}}M(\frac{n}{T})^{\ast}

naturally carries
right

g
-twisted vertex operators given by compositions
u^{\prime}\circ Y_{M}
(
u^{\prime}\in M^{\prime}
).
Such a structure induces an usual admissible
g^{-1}
-twisted module structure
Y_{M^{\prime}}
defined as

Y_{M^{\prime}}(a,z)u^{\prime}:=u^{\prime}\circ Y_{M}(e^{zL_{(1)}}(-z^{-2})^{L_{(0)}}a,z^{-1}),

(2.5)

where
a\in V
and
u^{\prime}\in M^{\prime}
. This module is called the
contragredient module
of
M
; refer to
Frenkel et al. 1993
;
Xu 1995
for more details.

Warning 2.8
.

For an admissible
g
-twisted module
M
, its components
M(\frac{n}{T})
need NOT be finite-dimensional. Consequently, its
double
contragredient module
M^{\prime\prime}
is not necessarily equal to
M
itself.
Hence, in general, given a
g^{-1}
-twisted module
N
, there is no guarantee that there exists an admissible
g
-twisted module
M
such that
M^{\prime}=N
.

Note that
2.5
implies

\left\langle Y_{M^{\prime}}(e^{zL_{(1)}}(-z^{-2})^{L_{(0)}}a,z^{-1})u^{\prime}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u\right\rangle=\left\langle u^{\prime}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}Y_{M}(a,z)u\right\rangle.

(2.6)

This allows us to spell out a translation between the
right
action of
\mathcal{L}_{g}(V)
and the
left
action of
\mathcal{L}_{g^{-1}}(V)
induced from the contragredient vertex operator
Y_{M^{\prime}}
.

Indeed, for any
a\in V
and
m\in\mathbb Z
, define

\theta(a({\textstyle\frac{m}{T}})):=\sum_{j\geqslant 0}\frac{(-1)^{\operatorname{wt}a}}{j!}(L_{(1)}^{j}a)({\textstyle\operatorname{wt}a-j-1+\deg a({\textstyle\frac{m}{T}})}).

(2.7)

Then,
\theta
is an anti-isomorphism between
\mathcal{L}_{g}(V)
and
\mathcal{L}_{g^{-1}}(V)
and we have

\theta(a({\textstyle\frac{m}{T}}))u^{\prime}=u^{\prime}\circ a_{(\frac{m}{T})},\qquad a\in V,u^{\prime}\in M^{\prime}.

(2.8)

In particular, we have

\theta(a({\textstyle\frac{m}{T}}))M^{\prime}(n)\subseteq M^{\prime}(n-\deg{a({\textstyle\frac{m}{T}})})

g
-twisted intertwining operators

The following definition can be found in
Xu 1995
:

Definition 2.9
.

Let
M^{1}
(resp.
M^{2}
and
M^{3}
) be a weak
untwisted
(resp.
g
-twisted
) module.
An
g
-twisted intertwining operator of type
\textstyle\binom{M^{3}}{M^{1}\;M^{2}}
is a linear map

I(\cdot,w)\colon M^{1}\longrightarrow\operatorname{Hom}(M^{2},M^{3})\{w\},\quad v\longmapsto I(v,w)=\sum_{m\in\mathbb C}v_{m}w^{-m-1},

satisfying the following axioms:

•

Truncation property
: For any
v\in M^{1}
,
v_{2}\in M^{2}
, and a fixed
\lambda\in\mathbb C
, we have

{v}_{\lambda+n}v_{2}=0

whenever
n\in\mathbb Q
and
n\gg 0
.

•

Twisted Jacobi identity
: For any
a\in V^{r}
and
v\in M^{1}
, we have

\displaystyle z_{1}^{-1}\operatorname{\delta}\left(\dfrac{z_{2}-w}{z_{1}}\right)Y_{M^{3}}(a,z_{2})I(v,w)-z_{1}^{-1}\operatorname{\delta}\left(\dfrac{w-z_{2}}{-z_{1}}\right)I(v,w)Y_{M^{2}}(a,z_{2})

(2.9)

\displaystyle=z_{2}^{-1}\left(\dfrac{w+z_{1}}{z_{2}}\right)^{r/T}\operatorname{\delta}\left(\dfrac{w+z_{1}}{z_{2}}\right)I(Y_{M^{1}}(a,z_{1})v,w).

•

L_{(-1)}
-derivative property
: For any
v\in M^{1}
,

I(L_{(-1)}v,w)=\frac{\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}}{\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{w}}I(v,w).

Denote the space of
g
-twisted intertwining operators of type
\textstyle\binom{M^{3}}{M^{1}\;M^{2}}
by
\mathfrak{I}\textstyle\binom{M^{3}}{M^{1}\;M^{2}}
and set

N\textstyle\binom{M^{3}}{M^{1}\;M^{2}}:=\dim\mathfrak{I}\textstyle\binom{M^{3}}{M^{1}\;M^{2}}.

These numbers are called the
fusion rules
associated to the above data.

The following proposition is a straightforward consequence of the
twisted Jacobi identity
and the
L_{(-1)}
-derivative property
. See
Frenkel et al. 1993
and
Frenkel and Zhu 1992
for more details.

Proposition 2.10
.

Suppose
M^{1}
,
M^{2}
, and
M^{3}
are of conformal weights
h_{1}
,
h_{2}
, and
h_{3}
respectively.
For any
v\in M^{1}
, we can write the intertwining operator
I(v,w)
as

w^{h_{1}+h_{2}-h_{3}}I(v,w)=\sum_{m\in\mathbb Z}v_{(\frac{m}{T})}z^{-\frac{m}{T}-1}\in\operatorname{Hom}(M^{2},M^{3})[\![{z^{\pm\frac{1}{T}}}]\!],

where
v_{(\frac{m}{T})}={v}_{h_{1}+h_{2}-h_{3}+\frac{m}{T}}
.
Furthermore,
v_{(\frac{m}{T})}M^{2}(\frac{n}{T})\subseteq M^{3}(\frac{n}{T}+\deg v-\frac{m}{T}-1)
for any homogeneous
v\in M^{1}
and any
m,n\in\mathbb N
.

Multiplying the
twisted Jacobi identity

2.9
with
z^{m+\frac{r}{T}}_{1}z_{2}^{h+\frac{n}{T}}z_{0}^{l}
, where
m,n,l\in\mathbb Z
, then take
\operatorname{Res}_{z_{0}}\operatorname{Res}_{z_{1}}\operatorname{Res}_{z_{2}}
, we obtain its component form:

Lemma 2.11
.

Let
a\in V^{r}
and
v\in M^{1}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i\geqslant 0}\binom{l}{i}(-1)^{i}a_{(\frac{r}{T}+m+l-i)}v_{(\frac{n}{T}+i)}-\sum_{i\geqslant 0}\binom{l}{i}(-1)^{l+i}v_{(\frac{n}{T}+l-i)}a_{(m+\frac{r}{T}+i)}

(2.10)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\geqslant 0}\binom{m+\frac{r}{T}}{j}(a_{(j+l)}v)_{(m-j+\frac{r+n}{T})},

for any
m,n,l\in\mathbb Z
.

2.2
Functions on the twisted projective line

Now we introduce the algebraic curve
\bar{C}
.
For the general theory of algebraic curves, we refer to various algebraic geometry textbooks such as
(
Liu 2002
, Ch. 7)
and
(
Hartshorne 1977
, Ch. IV)
, or consult
Fulton 2008
for a traditional treatment and
Schlag 2014
for an analytic approach to Riemann surfaces.

Throughout this paper and its subsequences, we adopt the following conventions on an integral scheme
(\mathfrak{X},\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}})
that is proper over
\mathbb C
:

•

We use the same notation for the analytification of
\mathfrak{X}
. According to
GAGA
, the categories of coherent
algebraic
sheaves on
\mathfrak{X}
and coherent
analytic
sheaves on
\mathfrak{X}
are equivalent, thus the terminology of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}}
-module
is unambiguous.
A special case provides the equivalence between
rational functions
and
meromorphic functions
on
\mathfrak{X}
, which allows us to use these terminologies interchangeably.
We use
\mathcal{K}_{\mathfrak{X}}
to denote the constant sheaf of the
field of rational functions
.

•

The
de Rham complex
of
\mathfrak{X}
is denoted as
(\Omega^{\bullet}_{\mathfrak{X}},\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{})
. When the
1
-forms
\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{x_{1}},\cdots,\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{x_{n}}
form a basis of the first de Rham cohomology space
H^{1}(\mathfrak{X},\Omega^{\bullet}_{\mathfrak{X}})
, we will use
\frac{\mathinner{\partial}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}}{\mathinner{\partial}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{x_{1}}},\cdots,\frac{\mathinner{\partial}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}}{\mathinner{\partial}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{x_{n}}}
to denote its dual basis in the module of derivatives.

•

Unless otherwise specified, a
point
of
\mathfrak{X}
, we mean a closed point of
\mathfrak{X}
. We use Fraktur letters, such as
\mathfrak{p}
and
\mathfrak{q}
, to denote such points. By abuse of notation, we do not distinguish a
skyscraper sheaf
supported at a point
\mathfrak{p}
with its stalk at
\mathfrak{p}
.
For a point
\mathfrak{p}
, we use
\mathcal{I}_{\mathfrak{p}}
to denote its ideal sheaf and
\kappa_{\mathfrak{p}}
the residue field
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}}/\mathcal{I}_{\mathfrak{p}}
.

•

Following
Frenkel and Ben-Zvi 2004
, we use
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{p}}
to denote the
complete local ring at
\mathfrak{p}
. That is the
\mathcal{I}_{\mathfrak{p}}
-adic completion
of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}}
, namely the limit
\varprojlim\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}}/\mathcal{I}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{n}
.
It is also the completion of the stalk of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}}
at
\mathfrak{p}
.

Now, we assume that
\mathfrak{X}
is a curve, i.e.,
\dim\mathfrak{X}=1
.

•

Each complete local ring
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{p}}
is a DVR. We use
v_{\mathfrak{p}}
to denote the normalized valuation (i.e.
v_{\mathfrak{p}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{p}}\setminus\{0\})=\mathbb N
).
This valuation extends to the function field
\mathcal{K}_{\mathfrak{X}}
.

•

A
divisor
on
\mathfrak{X}
is a linear combination of points of
\mathfrak{X}
.
The
support

\operatorname{supp}\Delta
of a divisor
\Delta
is the set of points involved (i.e. has nonzero coefficient) in
\Delta
.
Any rational function
f
on
\mathfrak{X}
defines a divisor
(f):=\sum_{\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{X}}v_{\mathfrak{p}}(f)\mathfrak{p}
.
Given a divisor
\Delta
, we use
\mathcal{O}(\infty\Delta)
to denote the sheaf of meromorphic functions with possible poles along
\Delta
.

•

By the
Cohen structure theorem
,
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{p}}\cong\mathbb C[\![{t}]\!]
for some topological generator
t
of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}}
.
Such an element
t
is called a
local coordinate at
\mathfrak{p}
.
The choice of
\mathfrak{p}
and
t
provides a morphism
\iota_{t}\colon\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}}\lhook\joinrel\rightarrow\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{p}}\cong\mathbb C[\![{t}]\!]
, images under which are called
formal expansions
.
The pair
(\mathfrak{p},t)
is thus called a
local chart
.

Formal expansions of rational functions

First, we recall the general formal expansions.

Lemma 2.12
.

Let
U
be an open neighborhood of a point
\mathfrak{p}
of
\mathfrak{X}
.
Then any rational function
f
on
U
admits an expansion

f=\sum_{n=v_{\mathfrak{p}}(f)}^{\infty}a_{n}t^{n},

where
a_{n}\in\mathbb C
and
t
is a fixed local coordinate at
\mathfrak{p}
.
This equality is understood in the sense that the series on the right-hand side converges to
f
under the
\mathcal{I}_{\mathfrak{p}}
-adic topology.

Proof.

When
f
is regular on
U
, this is just a concrete way to spell out the embedding
\iota_{t}
, where each
\sum_{n=0}^{m}a_{n}t^{n}
serves as a representative of the class
f+\mathcal{I}_{\mathfrak{p}}^{m+1}
.
The rational case follows by taking the fractional sections on both sides of the canonical embedding.
∎

To connect this lemma with its analytic counterpart, we need the following notions.

Definition 2.13
.

For a point
\mathfrak{p}
of
\mathfrak{X}
, a
(germ of)
1
-cycle around
\mathfrak{p}
is an element in the
costalk
of the
cosheaf
of punctured singular homology
U\mapsto H_{1}(U-\mathfrak{p},\mathbb Z)
, which can be presented as a
1
-cycle on a sufficiently small punctured neighborhood of
\mathfrak{p}
.
Such a
1
-cycle is called
simple
if its winding number is
1
.

Definition 2.14
.

For any meromorphic
1
-form
\alpha
on
\mathfrak{X}
, its
residue

\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}}\alpha
at a point
\mathfrak{p}\in\mathfrak{X}
is the value of the integration
\frac{1}{2\pi\mathbb{i}}\int_{\gamma}\alpha
, where
\gamma
is a simple
1
-cycle around
\mathfrak{p}
.

By this definition, given any rational map
f\colon\mathfrak{X}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
and any meromorphic
1
-form
\alpha
on
\mathbb{P}^{1}
, we have

\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}}f^{\ast}\alpha=\operatorname{mult}_{f}(\mathfrak{p})\cdot\operatorname{Res}_{f(\mathfrak{p})}\alpha.

(2.11)

Here
\operatorname{mult}_{f}(\mathfrak{p})
denotes the
multiplicity
of
f
at
\mathfrak{p}
, which is the
\kappa_{f(\mathfrak{p})}
-dimension of the fiber of the direct image
f_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{X}}
at
f(\mathfrak{p})
.

Lemma 2.15
.

The coefficient
a_{n}
in
Lemma

2.12
can be computed by the formula

a_{n}=\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}}t^{-n-1}f\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{t}.

Proof.

Direct computation shows

\int_{\gamma}t^{n}\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{t}=\begin{cases}2\pi\mathbb{i}&if $n=-1$,\\
0&otherwise,\end{cases}

where
\gamma
is a simple
1
-cycle around
\mathfrak{p}
.
Then, the statement follows.
∎

Remark 2.16
.

Let
t
be a local coordinate at
\mathfrak{p}
. The lemma shows the following: for any meromorphic function
f
, we have
\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}}f\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{t}=\operatorname{Res}_{t}(\iota_{t}f)
.

As a corollary of this lemma, the series in
Lemma

2.12
converges absolutely on
U-\mathfrak{p}
and defines a meromorphic function which can be expressed as the rational function
f
.

The following is a special case of
(
Frenkel and Ben-Zvi 2004
, Remark 9.2.10)
, originally credited to
Tate 1968
.

Lemma 2.17

(Strong Residue Theorem)
.

Let
{\mathfrak{p}}_{1},\cdots,{\mathfrak{p}}_{n}
be distinct points.
Then a collection of formal series
\left\{f_{i}\in\mathcal{O}_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}\right\}_{i=1,\cdots,n}
has the property that

\sum_{i=1}^{n}\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}_{i}}f\alpha=0,\qquad\text{for all}\qquad\alpha\in\Gamma(\mathfrak{X}-\{{\mathfrak{p}}_{1},\cdots,{\mathfrak{p}}_{n}\},\Omega^{1}),

if and only if
f_{i}
can be extended to the
same
regular function on
\mathfrak{X}-\{{\mathfrak{p}}_{1},\cdots,{\mathfrak{p}}_{n}\}
.

The twisted projective line

The algebraic curve we are concerned with is a
T
-twisted
version of the projective line. Abstractly, it is an
stacky curve
obtained from
\mathbb{P}^{1}
modulo an action of a cyclic group of order
T
.
We represent it as a smooth projective curve
\overline{C}
equipped with a ramified covering
\mathfrak{x}\colon\overline{C}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
, whose
Galois group
is cyclic of order
T
.

To make our expressions more explicit, we give the following
ad-hoc
construction.
First, let
C
be the smooth curve over
\mathbb C
defined by the polynomial

Y^{T}-X.

For a point
\mathfrak{p}\in C
, we use
(X(\mathfrak{p}),Y(\mathfrak{p}))
to denote its (global) coordinates in
\mathbb C^{2}
.
We refer to the point with coordinates
(X,Y)=(0,0)
as
0
.
Then, we have an isomorphism

\Gamma(C,\mathcal{O}_{C})=\mathbb C[X,Y]/(Y^{T}-X)\cong\mathbb C[z^{\frac{1}{T}}]\colon X\mapsto z,Y\mapsto z^{\frac{1}{T}}.

(2.12)

With the identification
2.12
, we have the following correspondences:

•

regular functions on
C

\longleftrightarrow

\mathbb C[z^{\frac{1}{T}}]
; and

•

regular functions on
C-\{0\}

\longleftrightarrow

\mathbb C[z^{\pm\frac{1}{T}}]
.

Next, we introduce
C^{\prime}
as another copy of
C
, with coordinates written as
X^{\prime}
and
Y^{\prime}
.
We refer to the point with
(X^{\prime},Y^{\prime})=(0,0)
as
\infty
.
Then, we can identify
C-\{0\}
with
C^{\prime}-\{\infty\}
through the isomorphism provided by
X^{-1}=X^{\prime}
and
Y^{-1}=Y^{\prime}
.
We call this domain
\accentset{\circ}{C}
.
Gluing
C
and
C^{\prime}
along
\accentset{\circ}{C}
results a compactification of
C
, denoted by
\overline{C}
.
Then,
2.12
extends to an isomorphism
\overline{C}\cong\operatorname{Proj}\mathbb C[z^{\frac{1}{T}}]
and gives the following correspondence:

•

rational functions on
\overline{C}

\longleftrightarrow

\mathbb C(z^{\frac{1}{T}})
.

Finally, the coordinate
X
extends to a rational function
\mathfrak{x}\colon\overline{C}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
provides the desired
T
-fold ramified covering
of the projective line
\mathbb{P}^{1}
, with
branch points

0,\infty
and
unramified locus

\mathring{\mathbb{P}}^{1}:=\mathbb{P}^{1}\setminus\{0,\infty\}
.
It is straightforward to verify that its group of
Deck transformations
is cyclic of order
T
.

The inverse image
\mathfrak{x}^{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}
can be interpreted as the subsheaf of
\mathcal{O}_{\overline{C}}
consisting of regular functions factoring through
\mathfrak{x}
.
Then, the following correspondence is straightforward:

•

rational functions on
\overline{C}
that factor through
\mathfrak{x}\colon\overline{C}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}

\longleftrightarrow

\mathbb C(z)\subseteq\mathbb C(z^{\frac{1}{T}})
.

On the other hand, the direct image
\mathfrak{x}_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{\overline{C}}
can be interpreted as an extension of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{P}^{1}}
.
Spelling out the stalks of
\mathfrak{x}_{\ast}\mathcal{O}_{\overline{C}}
, we see that its sections are multi-valued functions on
\mathbb{P}^{1}
.

Our construction leads to a natural choice of local coordinates at
0
,
\infty
, and
\mathfrak{p}\in\accentset{\circ}{C}
. Refer to
Table

1
for these coordinates, along with the established formal expansions provided by
Lemmas

2.12
and
2.15
.

a_{\frac{n}{T}}=\frac{1}{T}\operatorname{Res}_{0}Y^{-n}X^{-1}f\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{X}

(\infty,Y^{-1})

\displaystyle f=\sum_{n=-\infty}^{-v_{\infty}(f)}a_{\frac{n}{T}}Y^{n}

a_{\frac{n}{T}}=-\frac{1}{T}\operatorname{Res}_{\infty}Y^{-n}X^{-1}f\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{X}

(\mathfrak{p},X-X(\mathfrak{p}))

\displaystyle f=\sum_{n=v_{\mathfrak{p}}(f)}^{\infty}a_{n}(X-X(\mathfrak{p}))^{n}

\mathfrak{p}\in\overline{C}
.

The following corollary of
Lemma

2.17
plays a crucial role in the present work.

Lemma 2.18

(Residue Sum Formula)
.

For any meromorphic
1
-form
\alpha
on
\mathbb{P}^{1}
, we have

\tfrac{1}{T}\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}=0}\mathfrak{x}^{\ast}\alpha+\tfrac{1}{T}\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}=\infty}\mathfrak{x}^{\ast}\alpha+\sum_{\mathfrak{q}}\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}=\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{x}^{\ast}\alpha=0,

(2.13)

where
\mathfrak{q}
ranges over a branch of
\overline{C}
. That is to say, for each singularity
\bar{\mathfrak{q}}\in\mathbb{P}^{1}
of
\alpha
, we only take one representative
\mathfrak{q}
from
\mathfrak{x}^{-1}\bar{\mathfrak{q}}
.

Note that the summation is finite since
\alpha
only has finitely many poles.

Proof.

The
residue sum formula
on
\mathbb{P}^{1}
indicates that the sum of residues of a meromorphic
1
-form
\alpha
on
\mathbb{P}^{1}
is zero.
By
2.11
, we have

0=\sum_{\bar{\mathfrak{q}}\in\mathbb{P}^{1}}\operatorname{Res}_{\bar{\mathfrak{q}}}\alpha=\sum_{\mathfrak{q}}\operatorname{mult}_{\mathfrak{x}}(\mathfrak{q})^{-1}\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{q}}\mathfrak{x}^{\ast}\alpha,

where
\mathfrak{q}
ranges over a branch of
\overline{C}
.
Then
2.13
follows from the observation that
\operatorname{mult}_{\mathfrak{x}}(0)=T
,
\operatorname{mult}_{\mathfrak{x}}(\infty)=T
, and
\operatorname{mult}_{\mathfrak{x}}(\mathfrak{q})=1
for
\mathfrak{q}\in\accentset{\circ}{C}
.
∎

In the rest of this paper, we will frequently express a rational function on
\overline{C}
in terms of the local coordinate at the given point.
For simplicity, we introduce the following shorthand notations.

coordinate

Shorthand notations for coordinates.

Expansions of two-variable functions

Let
f(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{q})
be a meromorphic function on
\overline{C}\times\overline{C}
with possible poles at
0
,
\infty
, and the divisor
(z-w)
.
We can write
f(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{q})
in the form

f(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{q})=\frac{g(z^{\frac{1}{T}},w^{\frac{1}{T}})}{z^{\frac{m}{T}}w^{\frac{n}{T}}(z-w)^{l}},

where
g(z^{\frac{1}{T}},w^{\frac{1}{T}})\in\mathbb C[z^{\frac{1}{T}},w^{\frac{1}{T}}]
.
Fixing
\mathfrak{q}
and varying
\mathfrak{p}
, we can assign the following three expansions to
f
:

•

\iota_{\mathfrak{p}=\infty}f
, the formal expansion at the point
\infty
. This corresponds to the
\iota_{z,w}
-expansion
in formal calculus, reflecting that the series converges in the domain
\lvert z\rvert>\lvert w\rvert
.
By
Table

1
,

\iota_{\mathfrak{p}=\infty}f=\frac{g(z^{\frac{1}{T}},w^{\frac{1}{T}})}{z^{\frac{m}{T}}w^{\frac{n}{T}}}\sum_{i\geqslant 0}\binom{l+i-1}{i}z^{-l-i}w^{i}\in\mathbb C\lparen\!\lparen{z^{-\frac{1}{T}}}\rparen\!\rparen\lparen\!\lparen{w^{\frac{1}{T}}}\rparen\!\rparen.

(2.14)

In particular,
\operatorname{Res}_{z}(\iota_{\mathfrak{p}=\infty}f)=-\frac{1}{T}\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}=\infty}f\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}z\in\mathbb C[w^{\pm\frac{1}{T}}]
.

•

\iota_{\mathfrak{p}=0}f
, the formal expansion at the point
0
. This corresponds to the
\iota_{w,z}
-expansion
in formal calculus, reflecting that the series converges in the domain
\lvert w\rvert>\lvert z\rvert
.
By
Table

1
,

\iota_{\mathfrak{p}=0}f=\frac{g(z^{\frac{1}{T}},w^{\frac{1}{T}})}{z^{\frac{m}{T}}w^{\frac{n}{T}}}\sum_{i\geqslant 0}\binom{-l}{i}(-w)^{-l-i}z^{i}\in\mathbb C\lparen\!\lparen{w^{-\frac{1}{T}}}\rparen\!\rparen\lparen\!\lparen{z^{\frac{1}{T}}}\rparen\!\rparen.

(2.15)

In particular,
\operatorname{Res}_{z}(\iota_{\mathfrak{p}=0}f)=\frac{1}{T}\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}=0}f\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}z\in\mathbb C[w^{\pm\frac{1}{T}}]
.

•

\iota_{\mathfrak{p}=\mathfrak{q}}f
, the formal expansion at the point
\mathfrak{q}
. This corresponds to the
\iota_{w,z-w}
-expansion
in formal calculus, reflecting that the series converges in the domain
\lvert w\rvert>\lvert z-w\rvert
with the argument range
2
2

2

Here
\operatorname{Arg}(a:b)\in\mathopen{(}-\pi,\pi\mathclose{]}
denotes the argument of the ray
[a:b]\in\mathbb{P}^{1}
. This condition guarantees that
\mathfrak{p}
and
\mathfrak{q}
are in the same branch of
\overline{C}
.

\lvert\operatorname{Arg}(z^{\frac{1}{T}}:w^{\frac{1}{T}})\rvert<\frac{\pi}{2T}
.
By
Table

1
,

\iota_{\mathfrak{p}=\mathfrak{q}}z^{\frac{m}{T}}=\sum_{i\geqslant 0}\binom{\frac{m}{T}}{i}w^{\frac{m}{T}-i}(z-w)^{i}\in\mathbb C\lparen\!\lparen{w^{-\frac{1}{T}}}\rparen\!\rparen\lparen\!\lparen{z-w}\rparen\!\rparen.

(2.16)

In particular,
\operatorname{Res}_{z-w}(\iota_{\mathfrak{p}=\mathfrak{q}}f)=\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}=\mathfrak{q}}f\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}z\in\mathbb C[w^{\pm\frac{1}{T}}]
.

By
Proposition

2.10
and
2.9
, we have the following
duality property
.

Proposition 2.19
.

Let
M^{1}
be an admissible untwisted module, and let
M^{2}
and
M^{3}
be admissible
g
-twisted modules.
Suppose
M^{1}
,
M^{2}
, and
M^{3}
are of conformal weights
h_{1}
,
h_{2}
, and
h_{3}
respectively.
For any
a\in V^{r}
,
v\in M^{1}
,
v_{2}\in M^{2}
, and
v^{\prime}_{3}\in(M^{3})^{\prime}
, there exists a meromorphic function
f
on
\overline{C}\times\overline{C}
of the form (where
m,n,l\in\mathbb N
)

f(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{q})=\frac{g(z,w^{\frac{1}{T}})}{z^{\frac{r}{T}}z^{m}w^{\frac{n}{T}}(z-w)^{l}}\qquad(g(z,w^{\frac{1}{T}})\in\mathbb C[z,w^{\frac{1}{T}}]),

(2.17)

such that the following identities of formal Puiseux series hold:

\displaystyle\left\langle v^{\prime}_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}Y_{M^{3}}(a,z)I(v,w)v_{2}\right\rangle w^{h}=\iota_{\mathfrak{p}=\infty}f,

\displaystyle\left\langle v^{\prime}_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}I(v,w)Y_{M^{2}}(a,z)v_{2}\right\rangle w^{h}=\iota_{\mathfrak{p}=0}f,

\displaystyle\left\langle v^{\prime}_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}I(Y_{M^{1}}(a,z-w)v,w)v_{2}\right\rangle w^{h}=\iota_{\mathfrak{p}=\mathfrak{q}}f.

Proof.

With the formulas
2.14
,
2.15
, and
2.16
,
the statement follows from the the
twisted Jacobi identity

2.9
by the
Lemma 2.17

(Strong Residue Theorem)
.
.
∎

Conversely, we have

Proposition 2.20
.

For any meromorphic function on
\overline{C}\times\overline{C}
of the form
2.17
, we have

\operatorname{Res}_{z}\left(\iota_{\mathfrak{p}=\infty}z^{\frac{r}{T}}f\right)-\operatorname{Res}_{z}\left(\iota_{\mathfrak{p}=0}z^{\frac{r}{T}}f\right)=\operatorname{Res}_{z-w}\left(\iota_{\mathfrak{p}=\mathfrak{q}}z^{\frac{r}{T}}f\right).

In particular, the twisted Jacobi identity
2.9
of the intertwining operator
I
among an admissible untwisted module
M^{1}
and admissible
g
-twisted modules
M^{2}
and
M^{3}
is equivalent to the existence of a meromorphic function
f(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{q})
satisfying
Proposition

2.19
.

Proof.

Fixing
\mathfrak{q}\in\overline{C}
, the meromorphic
1
-form
z^{\frac{r}{T}}f\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{z}
factors through the covering map
x\colon\overline{C}\to\mathbb{P}^{1}
. Hence, the
Lemma 2.18

(Residue Sum Formula)
.
applies and gives us

\tfrac{1}{T}\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}=0}z^{\frac{r}{T}}f\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}z+\tfrac{1}{T}\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}=\infty}z^{\frac{r}{T}}f\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}z+\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}=\mathfrak{q}}z^{\frac{r}{T}}f\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}z=0.

Applying formulas
2.14
,
2.15
, and
2.16
to the above, we obtain the desired identity.
∎

2.3
Space of
g
-twisted correlation functions

In this subsection, we define the space of
g
-twisted correlation functions
for the
g
-twisted conformal blocks
.

Let
M^{1}
be an admissible untwisted module, and let
M^{2}
and
M^{3}
be admissible
g
-twisted modules. Suppose
M^{1}
,
M^{2}
, and
M^{3}
are of conformal weights
h_{1}
,
h_{2}
, and
h_{3}
, respectively. Suppose
I
is a
g
-twisted intertwining operator
in
\mathfrak{I}\textstyle\binom{M^{3}}{M^{1}\;M^{2}}
.
For
v\in M^{1}
,
v_{2}\in M^{2}
,
v_{3}^{\prime}\in(M^{3})^{\prime}
, and
a^{1}\in V^{r_{1}},\cdots,a^{n}\in V^{r_{n}}
, consider the following
(n+1)
-variable formal Puiseux series:

\left\langle v_{3}^{\prime}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}Y_{M^{3}}(a^{1},z_{1})\cdots Y_{M^{3}}(a^{k-1},z_{k-1})I(v,w)Y_{M^{2}}(a^{k},z_{k})\cdots Y_{M^{2}}(a^{n},z_{n})v_{2}\right\rangle w^{h},

(2.18)

Using a similar method as the proof of
(
Frenkel et al. 1993
, Proposition 3.5.1)
, by
Propositions

2.3
and
2.19
, applying the
Lemma 2.17

(Strong Residue Theorem)
.
, we see that there is a rational function on
\overline{C}^{n+1}
of the form:

f({\mathfrak{p}}_{1},\cdots,{\mathfrak{p}}_{n},\mathfrak{q})=\dfrac{\displaystyle g({z}_{1},\cdots,{z}_{n},w^{\frac{1}{T}})}{\displaystyle w^{\frac{n}{T}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}z_{i}^{\frac{r_{i}}{T}}z_{i}^{m_{i}}\prod_{j<k}(z_{j}-z_{k})^{l_{jk}}\prod_{p=1}^{n}(z_{p}-w)^{l_{p}}},

(2.19)

where
m_{i},n,l_{kj},l_{p}\in\mathbb N
, and
g({z}_{1},\cdots,{z}_{n},w^{\frac{1}{T}})\in\mathbb C[{z}_{1},\cdots,{z}_{n},w^{\frac{1}{T}}]
such that the series
2.18
is the formal expansion of
f({\mathfrak{p}}_{1},\cdots,{\mathfrak{p}}_{n},\mathfrak{q})
in the domain

\left\{({\mathfrak{p}}_{1},\cdots,{\mathfrak{p}}_{n},\mathfrak{q})\in\overline{C}^{n+1}\nonscript\>\middle|\allowbreak\nonscript\>\mathopen{}\infty>|z_{1}|>\cdots>|z_{k-1}|>|w|>|z_{k}|>\cdots>|z_{n}|>0\right\}.

We denote the space of functions of the form
2.19
by
\mathcal{F}({r}_{1},\cdots,{r}_{n})
.

Let
\Delta_{n}
denote the
divisor

\Delta_{n}:=(w\prod_{i=1}^{n}z_{i}\prod_{j<k}(z_{j}-z_{k})\prod_{p=1}^{n}(z_{p}-w)).

Then the space
\mathcal{O}(\infty\Delta_{n})
of meromorphic functions on
\overline{C}^{n+1}
with possible poles along the divisor
\Delta_{n}
, namely at the points where either
z_{i}=0
,
z_{i}=\infty
,
w=0
,
w=\infty
,
z_{j}=z_{k}
, or
z_{p}=w
, admits a decomposition

\mathcal{O}(\infty\Delta_{n})=\bigoplus_{0\leqslant{r}_{1},\cdots,{r}_{n}\leqslant T-1}\mathcal{F}({r}_{1},\cdots,{r}_{n}),

Note that
\mathcal{F}(\emptyset)=\mathcal{O}(\infty\Delta_{0})=\Gamma(\accentset{\circ}{C},\mathcal{O}_{\overline{C}})=\mathbb C[w^{\pm\frac{1}{T}}]
.

Following
Liu 2023
;
Zhu 1996
with slight modifications, we use the following notation to denote the function
f(\mathfrak{p}_{1},\cdots,\mathfrak{p}_{n},\mathfrak{q})
in
2.19
:

S_{I}\left\langle v_{3}^{\prime}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a^{1},\mathfrak{p}_{1})\cdots(a^{k-1},\mathfrak{p}_{k-1})(v,\mathfrak{q})(a^{k},\mathfrak{p}_{k})\cdots(a^{n},\mathfrak{p}_{n})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}v_{2}\right\rangle.

Then we obtain a system of linear maps
S_{I}=\left\{(S_{I})^{n}_{V\cdots M^{1}\cdots V}\right\}_{n\in\mathbb N}
, where

\displaystyle(S_{I})_{V\cdots M^{1}\cdots V}^{n}\colon

\displaystyle(M^{3})^{\prime}\otimes V\otimes\cdots\otimes V\otimes M^{1}\otimes V\otimes\cdots V\otimes M^{2}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}(\infty\Delta_{n}),

(2.20)

\displaystyle v_{3}^{\prime}\otimes a^{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes a^{k-1}\otimes v\otimes a^{k}\otimes\cdots\otimes a^{n}\otimes v_{2}

\displaystyle\longmapsto S_{I}\left\langle v_{3}^{\prime}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a^{1},\mathfrak{p}_{1})\cdots(a^{k-1},\mathfrak{p}_{k-1})(v,\mathfrak{q})(a^{k},\mathfrak{p}_{k})\cdots(a^{n},\mathfrak{p}_{n})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}v_{2}\right\rangle.

By
Propositions

2.3
and
2.19
, we have:

•

each map
(S_{I})_{V\cdots M^{1}\cdots V}^{n}
factors through
(M^{3})^{\prime}\otimes\operatorname{Sym}\left(V,\cdots,V,M^{1}\right)\otimes M^{2}
; and

•

(S_{I})^{n}_{M^{1}V\cdots V}=(S_{I})^{n}_{VM^{1}\cdots V}=\cdots=(S_{I})^{n}_{V\cdots VM^{1}}
.

Hence we can always put the terms in the order
(a^{1},\mathfrak{p}_{1})\cdots(a^{n},\mathfrak{p}_{n})(v,\mathfrak{q})
and omit these terms unless we want to emphasize some of them.

Note that for homogeneous
a^{1}\in V^{r_{1}},\cdots,a^{n}\in V^{r_{n}}
, the function

S_{I}\left\langle v_{3}^{\prime}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}\cdots\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}v_{2}\right\rangle z_{1}^{\frac{r_{1}}{T}}\cdots z_{n}^{\frac{r_{n}}{T}}

factors through
(x,\cdots,x,\operatorname{id})\colon\overline{C}^{n+1}\to(\mathbb{P}^{1})^{n}\times\overline{C}
, and thus can be viewed as a meromorphic function on
(\mathbb{P}^{1})^{n}\times\overline{C}
.
The following definition generalizes
(
Liu 2023
, Definition 2.1)
:

Definition 2.21
.

Let
V
be a VOA with an order
T
automorphism
g
, and let
M^{1}
(resp.
M^{2}
and
N^{3}
) be an admissible
untwisted
(resp.
g
-twisted
and
g^{-1}
-twisted
) module of conformal weight
h_{1}
(resp.
h_{2}
and
h_{3}
).
Put
h=h_{1}+h_{2}-h_{3}
.
A system of linear maps

S=\left\{S^{n}_{V\cdots M^{1}\cdots V}\right\}_{n\in\mathbb N}
,

\displaystyle S_{V\cdots M^{1}\cdots V}^{n}\colon

\displaystyle N^{3}\otimes V\otimes\cdots\otimes V\otimes M^{1}\otimes V\otimes\cdots V\otimes M^{2}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}(\infty\Delta_{n}),

\displaystyle v_{3}\otimes a^{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes a^{k-1}\otimes v\otimes a^{k}\otimes\cdots\otimes a^{n}\otimes v_{2}

\displaystyle\longmapsto S\left\langle v_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a^{1},\mathfrak{p}_{1})\cdots(a^{k-1},\mathfrak{p}_{k-1})(v,\mathfrak{q})(a^{k},\mathfrak{p}_{k})\cdots(a^{n},\mathfrak{p}_{n})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}v_{2}\right\rangle,

is said to satisfy the
twisted genus-zero property associated to the datum

\Sigma_{1}(N^{3},M^{1},M^{2}):=(\mathfrak{x}\colon\overline{C}\to\mathbb{P}^{1},\infty,1,0,N^{3},M^{1},M^{2})

(2.21)

if it satisfies the following axioms for all
v_{3}\in N^{3},v_{2}\in M^{2}
:

(i).

Truncation property
: For any fixed
v\in M^{1}
and
v_{2}\in M^{2}
, there exists
N\in\mathbb N
depending only on
v,v_{2}
, such that

S\left\langle v_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}v_{2}\right\rangle w^{\frac{N}{T}}\in\mathbb C[w^{\frac{1}{T}}]
for all
v_{3}\in N^{3}
.

(ii).

Locality
: The terms
(a^{1},\mathfrak{p}_{1})\cdots(a^{n},\mathfrak{p}_{n})(v,\mathfrak{q})
can be arbitrarily permuted:

•

each map
S_{V\cdots M^{1}\cdots V}^{n}
factors through
N^{3}\otimes\operatorname{Sym}\left(V,\cdots,V,M^{1}\right)\otimes M^{2}
; and

•

S^{n}_{M^{1}V\cdots V}=S^{n}_{VM^{1}\cdots V}=\cdots=S^{n}_{V\cdots VM^{1}}
.

Hence we can always put the terms in the order
(a^{1},\mathfrak{p}_{1})\cdots(a^{n},\mathfrak{p}_{n})(v,\mathfrak{q})
and omit these terms unless we want to emphasize some of them.

(iii).

Homogeneous property
: For homogeneous
a^{1}\in V^{r_{1}},\cdots,a^{n}\in V^{r_{n}}
, we have

S\left\langle v_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}\cdots\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}v_{2}\right\rangle\in\mathcal{F}({r}_{1},\cdots,{r}_{n}).

(2.22)

(iv).

Vacuum property
:

S\left\langle v_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(\mathbb{1},\mathfrak{p})\cdots\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}v_{2}\right\rangle=S\left\langle v_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}\cdots\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}v_{2}\right\rangle.

(2.23)

(v).

L_{(-1)}
-derivative property
:

\displaystyle\frac{\mathinner{\partial}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}}{\mathinner{\partial}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{z_{1}}}S\left\langle v_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a^{1},\mathfrak{p}_{1})\cdots\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}v_{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=S\left\langle v_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(L_{(-1)}a^{1},\mathfrak{p}_{1})\cdots\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}v_{2}\right\rangle,

(2.24)

\displaystyle\frac{\mathinner{\partial}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}}{\mathinner{\partial}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{w}}\left(S\left\langle v_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}\cdots(v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}v_{2}\right\rangle w^{-h}\right)

\displaystyle=S\left\langle v_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}\cdots(L_{(-1)}v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}v_{2}\right\rangle w^{-h}.

(2.25)

(vi).

Associativity
:

\displaystyle\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}=\mathfrak{q}}S\left\langle v_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a^{1},\mathfrak{p}_{1})\cdots(v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}v_{2}\right\rangle(z_{1}-w)^{k}\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{z_{1}}

\displaystyle=S\left\langle v_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}\cdots(a^{1}_{(k)}v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}v_{2}\right\rangle,

(2.26)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}=\mathfrak{p}_{2}}S\left\langle v_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a^{1},\mathfrak{p}_{1})(a^{2},\mathfrak{p}_{2})\cdots\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}v_{2}\right\rangle(z_{1}-z_{2})^{k}\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{z_{1}}

\displaystyle=S\left\langle v_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a^{1}_{(k)}a^{2},\mathfrak{p}_{2})\cdots\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}v_{2}\right\rangle.

(2.27)

(vii).

Generating property for
M^{2}
:
For any
a\in V^{r}
and
m\in\mathbb Z
, we have:

\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}=0}S\left\langle v_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a,\mathfrak{p})\cdots\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}v_{2}\right\rangle z^{m+\frac{r}{T}}\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{z}=TS\left\langle v_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}\cdots\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}a_{(m+\frac{r}{T})}v_{2}\right\rangle.

(2.28)

(viii).

Generating property for
N^{3}
:
For any
a\in V^{r}
and
m\in\mathbb Z
, we have

\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}=\infty}S\left\langle v_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a,\mathfrak{p})\cdots\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}v_{2}\right\rangle z^{m+\frac{r}{T}}\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{z}=-TS\left\langle\theta(a({\textstyle m+\frac{r}{T}}))v_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}\cdots\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}v_{2}\right\rangle,

(2.29)

where
\theta\colon\mathcal{L}_{g}(V)\to\mathcal{L}_{g^{-1}}(V)
is the anti-isomorphism defined in
2.7
and
\mathcal{L}_{g^{-1}}(V)
acts on the admissible
g^{-1}
-twisted module
N^{3}
via
Proposition

2.4
.

The vector space consists of systems of linear maps
S=\left\{S^{n}_{V\cdots M^{1}\cdots V}\right\}_{n\in\mathbb N}
satisfying the above axioms is called the
space of
g
-twisted correlation functions associated to the datum
\Sigma_{1}(N^{3},M^{1},M^{2})
, we denote it by
\operatorname{Cor}\left(\Sigma_{1}(N^{3},M^{1},M^{2})\right)
.
When
N^{3}
is the contragridient module of an admissible
g
-twisted module
M^{3}
, we call this space
the space of
g
-twisted correlation functions of type
\textstyle\binom{M^{3}}{M^{1}\;M^{2}}
and denote it by
\operatorname{Cor}\textstyle\binom{M^{3}}{M^{1}\;M^{2}}
.

Remark 2.22
.

Note that we do not initially require
N^{3}
to be the contragridient module of an admissible
g
-twisted module
M^{3}
in the definition above.

Proposition 2.23
.

The system
S_{I}
given by
2.20
belongs to
\operatorname{Cor}\textstyle\binom{M^{3}}{M^{1}\;M^{2}}
.

Proof.

We have already proven the
locality
(2) and the
homogeneous property
(3).
The properties (1), (4), and (5) follow from the
truncation property
, the
vacuum property
, and the
L_{(-1)}
-derivative property
of
I(\cdot,w)
and
Y_{M^{i}}(\cdot,z)
.

For the
associativity
(6): by
Propositions

2.3
and
2.19
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}=\mathfrak{q}}S_{I}\left\langle v_{3}^{\prime}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a^{1},\mathfrak{p}_{1})\cdots(v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}v_{2}\right\rangle(z_{1}-w)^{k}\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{z_{1}}

\displaystyle{=}\operatorname{Res}_{z_{1}-w}S_{I}\left\langle v_{3}^{\prime}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}\cdots(Y_{M^{1}}(a^{1},z_{1})v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}v_{2}\right\rangle(z_{1}-w)^{k}=S_{I}\left\langle v_{3}^{\prime}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}\cdots(a^{1}_{(k)}v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}v_{2}\right\rangle,

\displaystyle\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}=\mathfrak{p}_{2}}S_{I}\left\langle v_{3}^{\prime}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a^{1},\mathfrak{p}_{1})(a^{2},\mathfrak{p}_{2})\cdots\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}v_{2}\right\rangle(z_{1}-z_{2})^{k}\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{z_{1}}

\displaystyle{=}\operatorname{Res}_{z_{1}-z_{2}}S_{I}\left\langle v_{3}^{\prime}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(Y_{M^{1}}(a^{1},z_{1})a^{2},\mathfrak{p}_{2})\cdots\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}v_{2}\right\rangle(z_{1}-z_{2})^{k}=S_{I}\left\langle v_{3}^{\prime}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a^{1}_{(k)}a^{2},\mathfrak{p}_{2})\cdots\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}v_{2}\right\rangle.

For the
generating property
(7):

\displaystyle\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}=0}S_{I}\left\langle v_{3}^{\prime}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a,\mathfrak{p})\cdots\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}v_{2}\right\rangle z^{m+\frac{r}{T}}\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{z}=\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}=0}S_{I}\left\langle v_{3}^{\prime}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}\cdots(a,\mathfrak{p})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}v_{2}\right\rangle z^{m+\frac{r}{T}}\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{z}

\displaystyle\overset{\iota_{\mathfrak{p}=0}}{=}T\operatorname{Res}_{z}S_{I}\left\langle v_{3}^{\prime}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}\cdots\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}Y_{M^{2}}(a,z)v_{2}\right\rangle z^{m+\frac{r}{T}}=TS_{I}\left\langle v_{3}^{\prime}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}\cdots\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}a_{(m+\frac{r}{T})}v_{2}\right\rangle

For the
generating property
(8): using
2.6
,

\displaystyle\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}=\infty}S_{I}\left\langle v_{3}^{\prime}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a,\mathfrak{p})\cdots\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}v_{2}\right\rangle z^{m+\frac{r}{T}}\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{z}

\displaystyle{=}-T\operatorname{Res}_{z}S_{I}\left\langle Y_{(M^{3})^{\prime}}(e^{zL_{(1)}}(-z^{-2})^{L_{(0)}}a,z^{-1})v_{3}^{\prime}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}\cdots\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}v_{2}\right\rangle z^{m+\frac{r}{T}}\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{z}

\displaystyle{=}-TS_{I}\left\langle\theta(a({\textstyle m+\frac{r}{T}}))v_{3}^{\prime}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}\cdots\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}v_{2}\right\rangle.

Hence
S_{I}
satisfies the twisted genus-zero property associated to
\textstyle\binom{M^{3}}{M^{1}\;M^{2}}
.
∎

Now we have our first main theorem of this paper, which generalizes
(
Liu 2023
, Theorem 2.5 and Corollary 2.6)
.

Theorem 2.24
.

Let
M^{1}
(resp.
M^{2}
and
M^{3}
) be an admissible untwisted (resp.
g
-twisted) module of conformal weight
h_{1}
(resp.
h_{2}
and
h_{3}
).
Put
h=h_{1}+h_{2}-h_{3}
.
Then we have the following isomorphism of vector spaces:

\mathfrak{I}\textstyle\binom{M^{3}}{M^{1}\;M^{2}}\cong\operatorname{Cor}\textstyle\binom{M^{3}}{M^{1}\;M^{2}},\qquad I\longmapsto S_{I}.

Proof.

Given any
S\in\operatorname{Cor}\textstyle\binom{M^{3}}{M^{1}\;M^{2}}
, we define

I_{S}(\cdot,w)\colon M^{1}\to\operatorname{Hom}(M^{2},M^{3})[\![{w^{\pm\frac{1}{T}}}]\!]w^{-h},\quad v\mapsto I_{S}(v,w)=\sum_{n\in\mathbb Z}v_{(\frac{n}{T})}w^{-\frac{n}{T}-1-h},

where
v_{(\frac{n}{T})}
is determined by

\left\langle v_{3}^{\prime}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}v_{(\frac{n}{T})}v_{2}\right\rangle=\tfrac{1}{T}\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{q}=0}S\left\langle v_{3}^{\prime}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}v_{2}\right\rangle w^{\frac{n}{T}}\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{w}\in\mathbb C[w^{\pm\frac{1}{T}}],

(2.30)

where
v_{3}^{\prime}\in(M^{3})^{\prime}
and
v_{2}\in M^{2}
.
Then the
truncation property
and the
L_{(-1)}
-derivative property
of
I_{S}
follow from the axioms (1) and (5).
It remains to show the following
twisted Jacobi identity
of the twisted intertwining operator
I_{S}(\cdot,w)
:

\begin{multlined}\sum_{i\geqslant 0}\binom{l}{i}(-1)^{i}a_{(\frac{r}{T}+m+l-i)}v_{(\frac{n}{T}+i)}v_{2}-\sum_{i\geqslant 0}\binom{l}{i}(-1)^{l+i}v_{(\frac{n}{T}+l-i)}a_{(m+\frac{r}{T}+i)}v_{2}\\
=\sum_{j\geqslant 0}\binom{m+\frac{r}{T}}{j}(a_{(j+l)}v)_{(m-j+\frac{r+n}{T})}v_{2}.\end{multlined}

Note that the involved summations are finite due to the
truncation property
.

Indeed, by the
generating properties

2.29
,
2.30
, and
2.8
, we have

\displaystyle\left\langle v_{3}^{\prime}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}\sum_{i\geqslant 0}\binom{l}{i}(-1)^{i}a_{(\frac{r}{T}+m+l-i)}v_{(\frac{n}{T}+i)}v_{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\geqslant 0}\binom{l}{i}(-1)^{i}\left\langle\theta(a({\textstyle\frac{r}{T}+m+l-i}))v_{3}^{\prime}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}v_{(\frac{n}{T}+i)}v_{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\sum_{i\geqslant 0}\binom{l}{i}(-1)^{i}\tfrac{1}{T^{2}}\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{q}=0}\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}=\infty}S\left\langle v_{3}^{\prime}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a,\mathfrak{p})(v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}v_{2}\right\rangle z^{m+l-i-\frac{r}{T}}w^{\frac{n}{T}+i}\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{z}\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{w}

\displaystyle=-\tfrac{1}{T^{2}}\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{q}=0}\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}=\infty}S\left\langle v_{3}^{\prime}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a,\mathfrak{p})(v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}v_{2}\right\rangle(z-w)^{l}z^{m+\frac{r}{T}}w^{\frac{n}{T}}\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{z}\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{w}.

On the other hand, by
2.28
and
2.30
, we have

\displaystyle\left\langle v_{3}^{\prime}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}\sum_{i\geqslant 0}\binom{l}{i}(-1)^{l+i}v_{(\frac{n}{T}+l-i)}a_{(m+\frac{r}{T}+i)}v_{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\geqslant 0}\binom{l}{i}(-1)^{l+i}\tfrac{1}{T^{2}}\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{q}=0}\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}=0}S\left\langle v_{3}^{\prime}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a,\mathfrak{p})(v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}v_{2}\right\rangle z^{m+\frac{r}{T}+i}w^{\frac{n}{T}+l-i}\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{z}\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{w}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{T^{2}}\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{q}=0}\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}=0}S\left\langle v_{3}^{\prime}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a,\mathfrak{p})(v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}v_{2}\right\rangle(z-w)^{l}z^{m+\frac{r}{T}}w^{\frac{n}{T}}\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{z}\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{w}.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\left\langle v_{3}^{\prime}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}\sum_{i\geqslant 0}\binom{l}{i}(-1)^{i}a_{(\frac{r}{T}+m+l-i)}v_{(\frac{n}{T}+i)}v_{2}-\sum_{i\geqslant 0}\binom{l}{i}(-1)^{l+i}v_{(\frac{n}{T}+l-i)}a_{(m+\frac{r}{T}+i)}v_{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{T^{2}}\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{q}=0}(-\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}=\infty}-\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}=0})S\left\langle v_{3}^{\prime}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a,\mathfrak{p})(v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}v_{2}\right\rangle(z-w)^{l}z^{m+\frac{r}{T}}w^{\frac{n}{T}}\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{z}\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{w}

\displaystyle{=}\tfrac{1}{T}\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{q}=0}\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}=\mathfrak{q}}S\left\langle v_{3}^{\prime}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a,\mathfrak{p})(v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}v_{2}\right\rangle(z-w)^{l}z^{m+\frac{r}{T}}w^{\frac{n}{T}}\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{z}\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{w}

\displaystyle{=}\tfrac{1}{T}\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{q}=0}\sum_{j\geqslant 0}\binom{m+\frac{r}{T}}{j}\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}=\mathfrak{q}}S\left\langle v_{3}^{\prime}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a,\mathfrak{p})(v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}v_{2}\right\rangle(z-w)^{l+j}w^{m-j+\frac{r+n}{T}}\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{z}\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{w}

\displaystyle{=}\sum_{j\geqslant 0}\binom{m+\frac{r}{T}}{j}\tfrac{1}{T}\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{q}=0}S\left\langle v_{3}^{\prime}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a_{(l+j)}v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}v_{2}\right\rangle w^{m-j+\frac{r+n}{T}}\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{w}

\displaystyle{=}\sum_{j\geqslant 0}\binom{m+\frac{r}{T}}{j}\langle v_{3}^{\prime}\,|\,\mathopen{}(a_{(l+j)}v,\mathfrak{q})_{(m-j+\frac{r+n}{T})}v_{2}\rangle,

where
*
follows from the
residue sum formula
and
**
follows from applying
\iota_{\mathfrak{p}=\mathfrak{q}}
to
z^{m+\frac{r}{T}}
.
Indeed, we have
S\left\langle v_{3}^{\prime}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a,\mathfrak{p})(v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}v_{2}\right\rangle\in\mathcal{F}(r)
by the
homogeneous property
of
S
.
Hence the meromorphic
1
-form
S\left\langle v_{3}^{\prime}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a,\mathfrak{p})(v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}v_{2}\right\rangle(z-w)^{l}z^{m+\frac{r}{T}}w^{\frac{n}{T}}\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{z}
factors through
\mathfrak{x}
with possible poles
0
,
\infty
, and
w
on
\mathbb{P}^{1}
.
Then the
Lemma 2.18

(Residue Sum Formula)
.
applies.
For the expansion
\iota_{\mathfrak{p}=\mathfrak{q}}
, note that the summation involved is finite,
so it commutes with the integrals. Thus the equality follows.
∎

3
Reconstructing
g
-twisted correlation functions from restricted correlation functions

In this section, we introduce the space of
correlation functions
associated to the datum
(\mathfrak{x}\colon\overline{C}\to\mathbb{P}^{1},\infty,1,0,U^{3},M^{1},U^{2})
where
U^{2}
(resp.
U^{3}
) is a left (resp. right)
A_{g}(V)
-module.
In our application,
U^{2}
and
U^{3}
are the
lowest-weight
subspaces of
M^{2}
and
(M^{3})^{\prime}
respectively.
In general, they are only considered as irreducible modules over the
g
-twisted Zhu’s algebra

A_{g}(V)
.

Recall the definition of
A_{g}(V)
in
Dong et al. 1998
. It is the quotient of
V
modulo the subspace
O_{g}(V)
, which is spanned by

a\circ_{g}b:=\operatorname{Res}_{z}\frac{(1+z)^{\operatorname{wt}a-1+\delta(r)+\frac{r}{T}}}{z^{1+\delta(r)}}Y(a,z)b,\quad a\in V^{r},b\in V,

where
\delta(r)
is defined by

\delta(r)=\begin{cases}1&if $r=0$,\\
0&otherwise.\end{cases}

By
(
Dong et al. 1998
, Lemma 2.1)
,
V^{r}\subseteq O_{g}(V)
for
r\neq 0
.
Define

a\ast_{g}b:=\begin{cases}\operatorname{Res}_{z}Y(a,z)b\frac{(1+z)^{\operatorname{wt}a}}{z}&if $a\in V^{0}$,\\
0&otherwise.\end{cases}

(3.1)

Denote the image of
a\in V
in
A_{g}(V)
by
[a]
. We have the following result:

Lemma 3.1

(
Zhu 1996
;
Dong et al. 1998
)
.

The operation
\ast_{g}
induces an associative algebra structure on
A_{g}(V)
with
[\mathbb{1}]
as the identity, and
[\upomega]
lying in its center.

Let
M=\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbb N}M(\frac{n}{T})
be an admissible
g
-twisted module. By
2.2
, its bottom level
M(0)
is preserved by the
zero mode operators

o(a):=a_{(\operatorname{wt}a-1)}
(
a\in V
).
Note that the assignment
o\colon a\mapsto o(a)
vanishes outside
V^{0}
.
Furthermore, we have

Lemma 3.2

(
Zhu 1996
;
Dong et al. 1998
)
.

The bottom level
M(0)
is an
A_{g}(V)
-module with the action given by
[a]\cdot v=o(a)v
for
a\in V
and
v\in M(0)
. More specifically, we have

\displaystyle o(a\circ_{g}b)v

\displaystyle=0,

(3.2)

\displaystyle o(a)o(b)v

\displaystyle=o(a\ast b)v,

(3.3)

\displaystyle o(a)o(b)v-o(b)o(a)v

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\geqslant 0}\binom{\operatorname{wt}a-1}{j}o(a_{(j)}b)v\qquad\text{for }a\in V^{0}.

(3.4)

Given an
A_{g}(V)
-module
U
, the dual space
U^{\ast}
is a right module over
A_{g}(V)
,
where
[a]
acts on
u^{\prime}\in U^{\ast}
on the right by
\left\langle u^{\prime}\cdot[a]\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u\right\rangle=\left\langle u^{\prime}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}[a]\cdot u\right\rangle
for
u\in U
.
When
U=M(0)
for some admissible
g
-twisted module
M
, we have the following formula that is dual to
3.4
:

v^{\prime}o(a)o(b)-v^{\prime}o(b)o(a)=\sum_{j\geqslant 0}\binom{\operatorname{wt}a-1}{j}v^{\prime}o(a_{(j)}b),\quad a,b\in V^{0},v\in U,

3.1
Space of
g
-twisted restricted correlation functions

To define the auxiliary space
\operatorname{Cor}\left(\Sigma_{1}(U^{3},M^{1},U^{2})\right)
of
g
-twisted restricted correlation functions, we need the following two-variable functions on
\overline{C}

F_{n,i}(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{q}):=\frac{z^{-n}}{i!}\left(\frac{\mathinner{\partial}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}}{\mathinner{\partial}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{w}}\right)^{i}\frac{w^{n}}{z-w},

(3.5)

for all
n\in\tfrac{1}{T}\mathbb Z
.
The following lemmas are evident.

Lemma 3.3
.

The expansions of
F_{n,i}(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{q})
at
\mathfrak{p}=0
,
\mathfrak{p}=\infty
, and
\mathfrak{p}=\mathfrak{q}
are

\displaystyle\iota_{\mathfrak{p}=0}F_{n,i}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{j\geqslant 0}\binom{n-j-1}{i}z^{j-n}w^{n-j-i-1},

\displaystyle\iota_{\mathfrak{p}=\infty}F_{n,i}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\geqslant 0}\binom{n+j}{i}z^{-n-j-1}w^{n+j-i},

\displaystyle\iota_{\mathfrak{p}=\mathfrak{q}}F_{n,i}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=0}^{i}\sum_{p\geqslant 0}\binom{n}{i-l}\binom{-n}{p}w^{-i+l-p}(z-w)^{p-l-1}.

Lemma 3.4
.

The functions
F_{n,i}(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{q})
for successive
n
have the following relation:

F_{n,i}(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{q})-F_{n+1,i}(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{q})=\binom{n}{i}z^{-n-1}w^{n-i}.

We now give a definition that generalizes
(
Liu 2023
, Definition 3.1)
to the
g
-twisted case. Note that there is an additional shifting in each recursive formula.

Definition 3.5
.

Let
M^{1}
be an admissible untwisted module of conformal weight
h_{1}
, and
U^{2}
(resp.
U^{3}
) a left (resp. right)
A_{g}(V)
-module where
[\upomega]
acts as
h_{2}\operatorname{id}
(resp.
h_{3}\operatorname{id}
). Put
h=h_{1}+h_{2}-h_{3}
.
A system of linear maps
S=\left\{S^{n}_{V\cdots M^{1}\cdots V}\right\}_{n\in\mathbb N}
, where

\displaystyle S_{V\cdots M^{1}\cdots V}^{n}\colon

\displaystyle U^{3}\otimes V\otimes\cdots\otimes V\otimes M^{1}\otimes V\otimes\cdots V\otimes U^{2}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}(\infty\Delta_{n}),

\displaystyle u_{3}\otimes a^{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes a^{k-1}\otimes v\otimes a^{k}\otimes\cdots\otimes a^{n}\otimes u_{2}

\displaystyle\longmapsto S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a^{1},\mathfrak{p}_{1})\cdots(a^{k-1},\mathfrak{p}_{k-1})(v,\mathfrak{q})(a^{k},\mathfrak{p}_{k})\cdots(a^{n},\mathfrak{p}_{n})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle,

is said to satisfy the
twisted genus-zero property associated to the datum

\Sigma_{1}(U^{3},M^{1},U^{2}):=(\mathfrak{x}\colon\overline{C}\to\mathbb{P}^{1},\infty,1,0,U^{3},M^{1},U^{2})

(3.6)

if it satisfies the following axioms for all
u_{2}\in U^{2}
and
u_{3}\in U^{3}
:

(i).

Properties (2)–(6) in
Definition

2.21
, with
u_{3}\in U^{3}
and
u_{2}\in U^{2}
.

(ii).

Monomial property
: There is a linear functional
\varphi\in(U^{3}\otimes M^{1}\otimes U^{2})^{\ast}
such that

S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle=\left\langle\varphi\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{3}\otimes w^{-L(0)+h_{1}}v\otimes u_{2}\right\rangle.

(3.7)

(iii).

Recursive formula about
U^{3}
and
V
: For any
a\in V^{r}
, we have

\displaystyle S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a,\mathfrak{p})\cdots\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=S\left\langle u_{3}\cdot[a]\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}\cdots\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle z^{-\operatorname{wt}a}

(3.8)

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sum_{i\geqslant 0}F_{\operatorname{wt}a-1+\delta(r)+\frac{r}{T},i}(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}_{k})S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}\cdots(a_{(i)}a^{k},\mathfrak{p}_{k})\cdots\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\sum_{i\geqslant 0}F_{\operatorname{wt}a-1+\delta(r)+\frac{r}{T},i}(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{q})S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}\cdots(a_{(i)}v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle,

(iv).

Recursive formula about
U^{2}
and
V
: For any
a\in V^{r}
, we have

\displaystyle S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}\cdots(a,\mathfrak{p})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}\cdots\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}[a]\cdot u_{2}\right\rangle z^{-\operatorname{wt}a}

(3.9)

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=1}^{n}\sum_{i\geqslant 0}F_{\operatorname{wt}a-1+\frac{r}{T},i}(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{p}_{k})S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}\cdots(a_{(i)}a^{k},\mathfrak{p}_{k})\cdots\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\sum_{i\geqslant 0}F_{\operatorname{wt}a-1+\frac{r}{T},i}(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{q})S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}\cdots(a_{(i)}v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle,

The vector space consists of systems of linear maps
S=\left\{S^{n}_{V\cdots M^{1}\cdots V}\right\}_{n\in\mathbb N}
satisfying the above axioms is called the
space of
g
-twisted restricted correlation functions associated to the datum
\Sigma_{1}(U^{3},M^{1},U^{2})
and is denoted by
\operatorname{Cor}\left(\Sigma_{1}(U^{3},M^{1},U^{2})\right)
.

Proposition 3.6
.

Let
S
be a system of
g
-twisted correlation functions associated to the datum
\Sigma_{1}(N^{3},M^{1},M^{2})
in
2.21
, then its restriction to the bottom levels of
N^{3}
and
M^{2}
gives a system of
g
-twisted restricted correlation functions associated to the datum
\Sigma_{1}(N^{3}(0),M^{1},M^{2}(0))
.

Proof.

We first show the
monomial property
.
Since we have
S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle\in\mathbb C[w^{\pm\frac{1}{T}}]
by
truncation property
, it suffics to show the
w
-derivative of
