is completed.
∎

4.3
The
(n+3)
-point correlation functions

We can use a similar induction argument as in
(
Liu 2023
, Section 4.3)
to construct the
(n+3)
-point function
S
, with the well-defineness and locality of the
3
-point,
4
-point, and
5
-point functions from the previous subsection as the base case. Note that the only property involving the
A(V)
-modules
U^{2}
and
U^{3}
we used for the induction process in
Liu 2023
was

\displaystyle S\left\langle u_{3}\cdot[a^{1}][a^{2}]\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a^{3},\mathfrak{p}_{3})\cdots(a^{n},\mathfrak{p}_{n})(v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle-S\left\langle u_{3}\cdot[a^{2}][a^{1}]\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a^{3},\mathfrak{p}_{3})\cdots(a^{n},\mathfrak{p}_{n})(v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sum_{j\geqslant 0}\binom{\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-1}{j}S\langle u_{3}\cdot[a^{1}_{(j)}a^{2}]\,|\,\mathopen{}(a^{3},\mathfrak{p}_{3})\cdots(a^{n},\mathfrak{p}_{n})(v,\mathfrak{q})\,|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\rangle,

which is also true when
U^{2}
and
U^{3}
are modules over
A_{g}(V)
. We omit the rest of the details for the induction.

Thus, we have a well-defined system of
(n+3)
-point functions for
n\geqslant 3
.

\displaystyle S_{V\cdots M\cdots V}\colon U^{3}\otimes V\otimes\cdots\otimes M^{1}

\displaystyle\otimes\cdots V\otimes U^{2}\rightarrow\mathcal{O}(\infty\Delta_{n})

(4.25)

\displaystyle u_{3}\otimes a^{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes v\cdots\otimes a^{n}\otimes u_{2}

\displaystyle\mapsto S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a_{1},\mathfrak{p}_{1})\cdots(v,\mathfrak{q})\cdots(a^{n},\mathfrak{p}_{n})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle,

where
u_{3}\in U^{3}
,
a^{1},\cdots a^{n}\in V
,
v\in M^{1}
, and
u_{2}\in U^{2}
. Note that
S
satisfies the recursive formulas
3.9
and
3.8
, and the
locality
in
Definition

3.5
.

5
Associativity of the reconstructed correlation functions

In this section, we show that the system of
(n+3)
-point functions
S
we constructed in
Section

4
is contained in
\operatorname{Cor}\left(\Sigma_{1}(U^{3},M^{1},U^{2})\right)
.

By our construction in
Section

4
, it remains to show the
associativity
in
Definition

3.5
. Since the recursive formulas for the correlation functions in
Definition

3.5
are different from the ones in
Liu 2023
, there are
5
new cases arise in our case. Recall there are two formulas for the associativity: for any
k\in\mathbb Z
,

\displaystyle\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}=\mathfrak{q}}S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a^{1},\mathfrak{p}_{1})\cdots(v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle(z_{1}-w)^{k}\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{z_{1}}

\displaystyle=S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}\cdots(a^{1}_{(k)}v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle,

(5.1)

\displaystyle\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}=\mathfrak{p}_{2}}S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a^{1},\mathfrak{p}_{1})(a^{2},\mathfrak{p}_{2})\cdots\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle(z_{1}-z_{2})^{k}\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{z_{1}}

\displaystyle=S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a^{1}_{(k)}a^{2},\mathfrak{p}_{2})\cdots\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle.

(5.2)

5.1
Associativity for one algebra element and one module element

We first prove
5.1
for the
4
-point functions. The general
(n+3)
-point functions case can be proved in a similar way, so we omit the details.

Proposition 5.1
.

For
a^{1}\in V
,
u_{3}\in U^{3}
,
u_{2}\in U^{2}
,
v\in M^{1}
homogeneous, and
k\in\mathbb Z
, we have

\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}=\mathfrak{q}}S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a^{1},\mathfrak{p}_{1})(v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle(z_{1}-w)^{k}\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{z_{1}}=S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a^{1}_{(k)}v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle.

(5.3)

Proof.

Suppose
a^{1}\in V^{r}
. When
k\geqslant 0
, by
Lemmas

3.3
,
4.6
, and
4.7
,

\displaystyle\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}=\mathfrak{q}}S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a^{1},\mathfrak{p}_{1})(v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle(z_{1}-w)^{k}\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{z_{1}}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}=\mathfrak{q}}\left\langle\varphi\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{3}\otimes{v}\otimes[a^{1}]\cdot u_{2}\right\rangle w^{-\deg v}z_{1}^{-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}}(z_{1}-w)^{k}\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{z_{1}}

\displaystyle+\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}=\mathfrak{q}}\sum_{i\geqslant 0}F_{\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-1+\frac{r}{T},i}(\mathfrak{p}_{1},\mathfrak{q})\left\langle\varphi\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{3}\otimes{a^{1}_{(i)}v}\otimes u_{2}\right\rangle w^{-(\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-i-1+\deg v)}(z_{1}-w)^{k}\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{z_{1}}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}=\mathfrak{q}}\sum_{j\geqslant 0}\binom{-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}}{j}w^{-\deg v-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-j}(z_{1}-w)^{j+k}\left\langle\varphi\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{3}\otimes{v}\otimes[a^{1}]\cdot u_{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}=\mathfrak{q}}\sum_{i\geqslant 0}\sum_{l=0}^{i}\sum_{p\geqslant 0}\binom{\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-1+\frac{r}{T}}{i-l}\binom{-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}+1-\frac{r}{T}}{p}\frac{w^{l-p-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}+1-\deg v}}{(z_{1}-w)^{l+1-p-k}}

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\left\langle\varphi\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{3}\otimes{a^{1}_{(i)}v}\otimes u_{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sum_{i\geqslant 0}\left(\sum_{l=k}^{i}\binom{\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-1+\frac{r}{T}}{i-l}\binom{-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}+1-\frac{r}{T}}{l-k}\right)w^{k-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}+1-\deg v}\left\langle\varphi\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{3}\otimes{a^{1}_{(i)}v}\otimes u_{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\sum_{i\geqslant 0}\left(\sum_{s=0}^{i-k}\binom{\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-1+\frac{r}{T}}{i-k-s}\binom{-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}+1-\frac{r}{T}}{s}\right)w^{k-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}+1-\deg v}\left\langle\varphi\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{3}\otimes{a^{1}_{(i)}v}\otimes u_{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\left\langle\varphi\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{3}\otimes{a^{1}_{(k)}v}\otimes u_{2}\right\rangle w^{k-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}+1-\deg v}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a^{1}_{(k)}v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle,

where we used the fact that
\sum_{s=0}^{i-k}\binom{\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-1+\tfrac{r}{T}}{i-k-s}\binom{-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}+1-\tfrac{r}{T}}{s}
is the coefficient of the term
x^{i-k}
in
(1+x)^{\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-1+\frac{r}{T}}(1+x)^{-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}+1-\frac{r}{T}}=1
.

When
k=-1
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}=\mathfrak{q}}S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a^{1},\mathfrak{p}_{1})(v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle(z_{1}-w)^{-1}\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{z_{1}}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\left\langle\varphi\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{3}\otimes{v}\otimes[a^{1}]\cdot u_{2}\right\rangle w^{-\deg v-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}}

(5.4)

\displaystyle+\sum_{i\geqslant 0}\left(\sum_{l=0}^{i}\binom{\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-1+\frac{r}{T}}{i-l}\binom{-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}+1-\frac{r}{T}}{l+1}\right)w^{-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-\deg v}\left\langle\varphi\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{3}\otimes{a^{1}_{(i)}v}\otimes u_{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\left\langle\varphi\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{3}\otimes{v}\otimes[a^{1}]\cdot u_{2}\right\rangle w^{-\deg v-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}}

\displaystyle-\sum_{i\geqslant 0}\binom{\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-1+\frac{r}{T}}{i+1}w^{-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-\deg v}\left\langle\varphi\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{3}\otimes{a^{1}_{(i)}v}\otimes u_{2}\right\rangle.

If
r=0
, by
4.3
we have

\lx@cref{refnum}{5.4}=\binom{\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-1}{0}\left\langle\varphi\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{3}\otimes{a^{1}_{(-1)}v}\otimes u_{2}\right\rangle w^{-\deg v-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}}=S\left\langle v^{\prime}_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a^{1}_{(-1)}v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle.

If
r\neq 0
, since
[a^{1}]=0
, by
4.4
we have

\lx@cref{refnum}{5.4}=\binom{\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-1+\frac{r}{T}}{0}w^{-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-\deg v}\left\langle\varphi\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{3}\otimes{a^{1}_{(-1)}v}\otimes u_{2}\right\rangle=S\left\langle v^{\prime}_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a^{1}_{(-1)}v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle.

When
k<-1
, the proof is similar to the proof of
(
Zhu 1996
, (2.2.9))
, using the
L_{(-1)}
-derivative property
in
Proposition

4.12
, we omit the details.
∎

5.2
Associativity for two algebra elements

In this subsection, we prove
5.2
for the
5
-point functions. The general
(n+3)
-point functions case can be proved similarly.
First, we show that the kernel
J
of conformal blocks in
Definition

4.1
also contains some information about a generalized version of
O(M^{1})
in
Frenkel and Zhu 1992
;
Liu 2023
. We give the following definition with the notations in
Dong et al. 1998
;
Jiang and Jiao 2016
:

Definition 5.2
.

Let
(M,Y_{M})
be an untwisted module, and
\lambda
be a complex number. Introduce a bilinear operator
\circ_{g}:V\otimes M\longrightarrow M
by letting

\displaystyle a\circ_{g}v:

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Res}_{x}\frac{(1+x)^{\operatorname{wt}a-1+\delta(r)+\frac{r}{T}}}{x^{1+\delta(r)}}Y_{M}(a,x)v,

(5.5)

where
0\leqslant r\leqslant T-1
,
a\in V^{r}
homogeneous, and
v\in M
. Let

O_{g,\lambda}(M):=\operatorname{span}\left\{a\circ_{g}u,\ L_{(-1)}u+(L_{(0)}+\lambda)u:a\in V,u\in M\right\},

(5.6)

and
B_{g,\lambda}(M):=M/O_{g,\lambda}(M)
.

We will show that
B_{g,\lambda}(M)
is a bimodule over the
g
-twisted Zhu’s algebra
A_{g}(V)
in the next Section. The following property is necessary for the proof of associativity
5.2
.

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
J
be the subspace spanned by elements of the form
4.1
-
4.4
in
Definition

4.1
. Then,
U^{3}\otimes O_{g,h_{2}-h_{3}}(M^{1})\otimes U^{2}\subseteq J
.

Proof.

By
4.1
,
4.4
, and
5.5
, it is clear that
u_{3}\otimes(L_{(-1)}u+(L_{(0)}+h_{2}-h_{3})u)\otimes u_{2}\in J
, and
u_{3}\otimes(b\circ_{g}u)\otimes u_{2}\in J
, where
b\in V^{r}
with
1\leqslant t\leqslant T-1
. Now let
a\in V^{0}
. Since
[L_{(-1)}a+L_{(0)}a]=0
, it follows from
4.2
that

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=u_{3}\cdot[L_{(-1)}+L_{(0)}a]\otimes u\otimes u_{2}

\displaystyle\equiv-\sum_{j\geqslant 0}\binom{\operatorname{wt}a+1}{j}u_{3}\otimes(L_{(-1)}a)_{(j-1)}u\otimes u_{2}-\sum_{j\geqslant 0}\binom{\operatorname{wt}a}{j}u_{3}\otimes(L_{(0)}a)_{(j-1)}u\otimes u_{2}

\displaystyle\equiv-u_{3}\otimes\left((L_{(-1)}a+L_{(0)}a)\ast u\right)\otimes u_{2}\equiv u_{3}\otimes(a\circ_{g}u)\otimes u_{2}\pmod{J}.

Thus
u_{3}\otimes O_{g,h_{2}-h_{3}}(M^{1})\otimes u_{2}\subseteq J
, in view of
5.6
.
∎

By
Lemma

5.3
, together with the
L_{(-1)}
-derivative property
of module
M^{1}
, it is easy to show the following fact (see
(
Zhu 1996
, Lemma 2.1.2)
):

u_{3}\otimes\left(\operatorname{Res}_{x}\frac{(1+x)^{\operatorname{wt}a-1+\delta(r)+\frac{r}{T}+i}}{x^{j+1+\delta(r)}}Y_{M^{1}}(a,x)v\right)\otimes u_{2}\in J,\quad j\geqslant i\geqslant 0,i,j\in\mathbb N.

(5.7)

Lemma 5.4
.

Let
S
be the
3
-point function in
4.6
. For
a\in V^{r}
homogeneous,
v\in M^{1}
,
u_{2}\in U^{2}
,
u_{3}\in U^{3}
, and
j\in\mathbb N
, we have

\operatorname{Res}_{x}\frac{(w+x)^{\operatorname{wt}a-1+\delta(r)+\frac{r}{T}}}{x^{j+\delta(r)}}S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(Y_{M^{1}}(a,x)v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle=\begin{cases}S\left\langle u_{3}\cdot[a]\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle&if $r,j=0$,\\
0&if $j\geqslant 1$.\end{cases}

(5.8)

Proof.

By
4.6
and the change of variable formula, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Res}_{x}\frac{(w+x)^{\operatorname{wt}a-1+\delta(r)+\frac{r}{T}}}{x^{j+\delta(r)}}S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(Y_{M^{1}}(a,x)v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Res}_{x}\frac{(w+x)^{\operatorname{wt}a-1+\delta(r)+\frac{r}{T}}}{x^{j+\delta(r)}}\sum_{n\in\mathbb Z}\left\langle\varphi\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{3}\otimes a_{(n+\frac{r}{T})}v\otimes u_{2}\right\rangle x^{-n-\frac{r}{T}-1}w^{-\operatorname{wt}a+n+\frac{r}{T}+1-\deg v}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Res}_{x}\frac{1}{w}\frac{(1+x/w)^{\operatorname{wt}a-1+\delta(r)+\frac{r}{T}}}{(x/w)^{j+\delta(r)}}\left\langle\varphi\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{3}\otimes Y_{M^{1}}(a,x/w)v\otimes u_{2}\right\rangle w^{-j-\deg v-\frac{r}{T}}

\displaystyle=\operatorname{Res}_{z}\frac{(1+z)^{\operatorname{wt}a-1+\delta(r)+\frac{r}{T}}}{z^{j+\delta(r)}}\left\langle\varphi\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{3}\otimes Y_{M^{1}}(a,z)v\otimes u_{2}\right\rangle w^{-j-\deg v-\frac{r}{T}}.

By
5.7
, the last term is
0
if
j\geqslant 1
. On the other hand, if
r,j=0
, by
4.2
we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Res}_{z}\frac{(1+z)^{\operatorname{wt}a}}{z}\left\langle\varphi\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{3}\otimes Y_{M^{1}}(a,z)v\otimes u_{2}\right\rangle w^{-\deg v}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\varphi\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{3}\otimes\sum_{i\geqslant 0}\binom{\operatorname{wt}a}{i}a_{(i-1)}v\otimes u_{2}\right\rangle w^{-\deg v}

\displaystyle=\left\langle\varphi\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{3}\cdot[a]\otimes v\otimes u_{2}\right\rangle w^{-\deg v}=S\left\langle u_{3}\cdot[a]\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle.

This proves
5.8
.
∎

Proposition 5.5
.

For any
u_{3}\in U^{3}
,
u_{2}\in U^{2}
,
v\in M^{1}
,
a^{1},a^{2}\in V
, and
k\in\mathbb Z
, we have

\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}=\mathfrak{p}_{2}}S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a^{1},\mathfrak{p}_{1})(a^{2},\mathfrak{p}_{2})(v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle(z_{1}-z_{2})^{k}\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{z_{1}}=S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a^{1}_{(k)}a^{2},\mathfrak{p}_{2})(v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle.

(5.9)

Proof.

It suffices to prove
Proposition

5.5
for homogeneous
a^{1}\in V^{r}
and
a^{2}\in V^{s}
, where
0\leqslant r,s<T
. Note that
a^{1}_{(n)}a^{2}\in V^{\overline{r+s}}
for any
n\in\mathbb Z
, where
\overline{r+s}
denotes the residue of
r+s
modulo
T
.

When
k\geqslant 0
, by
4.8
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}=\mathfrak{p}_{2}}S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a^{1},\mathfrak{p}_{1})(a^{2},\mathfrak{p}_{2})(v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle(z_{1}-z_{2})^{k}\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{z_{1}}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}=\mathfrak{p}_{2}}S\left\langle u_{3}\cdot[a^{1}]\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a^{2},\mathfrak{p}_{2})(v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle z_{1}^{-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}}(z_{1}-z_{2})^{k}\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{z_{1}}

\displaystyle+\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}=\mathfrak{p}_{2}}\sum_{i\geqslant 0}F_{\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-1+\delta(r)+\frac{r}{T}}(\mathfrak{p}_{1},\mathfrak{p}_{2})S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a^{1}_{(i)}a^{2},\mathfrak{p}_{2})(v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle(z_{1}-z_{2})^{k}\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{z_{1}}

\displaystyle+\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}=\mathfrak{p}_{2}}\sum_{i\geqslant 0}F_{\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-1+\delta(r)+\frac{r}{T}}(\mathfrak{p}_{1},\mathfrak{q})S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a^{2},\mathfrak{p}_{2})(a^{1}_{(i)}v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle(z_{1}-z_{2})^{k}\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{z_{1}}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle 0+\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}=\mathfrak{p}_{2}}\sum_{i\geqslant 0}\sum_{l=0}^{i}\sum_{p\geqslant 0}\binom{\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-1+\delta(r)+\frac{r}{T}}{i-l}\binom{-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}+1-\delta(r)-\frac{r}{T}}{p}

\displaystyle\cdot\frac{z_{2}^{-i+l-p}}{(z_{1}-z_{2})^{l+1-p-k}}S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a^{1}_{(i)}a^{2},\mathfrak{p}_{2})(v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle+0

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\geqslant 0}\left(\sum_{l=k}^{i}\binom{\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-1+\delta(r)+\frac{r}{T}}{i-l}\binom{-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}+1-\delta(r)-\frac{r}{T}}{l-k}\right)

\displaystyle\cdot S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a^{1}_{(i)}a^{2},\mathfrak{p}_{2})(v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle z_{2}^{-i+k}

\displaystyle=S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a^{1}_{(k)}a^{2},\mathfrak{p}_{2})(v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle.

Now consider the case where
k=-1
. Similar to the case
k\geqslant 0
, we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}=\mathfrak{p}_{2}}S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a^{1},\mathfrak{p}_{1})(a^{2},\mathfrak{p}_{2})(v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle(z_{1}-z_{2})^{-1}\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{z_{1}}-S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a^{1}_{(-1)}a^{2},\mathfrak{p}_{2})(v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}=\mathfrak{p}_{2}}S\left\langle u_{3}\cdot[a^{1}]\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a^{2},\mathfrak{p}_{2})(v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle z_{1}^{-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}}(z_{1}-z_{2})^{-1}\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{z_{1}}

\displaystyle+\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}=\mathfrak{p}_{2}}\sum_{i\geqslant 0}F_{\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-1+\delta(r)+\frac{r}{T},i}(\mathfrak{p}_{1},\mathfrak{p}_{2})S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a^{1}_{(i)}a^{2},\mathfrak{p}_{2})(v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle(z_{1}-z_{2})^{-1}\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{z_{1}}

\displaystyle+\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}=\mathfrak{p}_{2}}\operatorname{Res}_{x_{1}}\frac{z_{1}^{-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}+1-\delta(r)-\frac{r}{T}}(w+x_{1})^{\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-1+\delta(r)+\frac{r}{T}}}{z_{1}-w-x_{1}}(z_{1}-z_{2})^{-1}\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{z_{1}}

\displaystyle\cdot S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a^{2},\mathfrak{p}_{2})(Y_{M^{1}}(a^{1},x_{1})v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle-S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a^{1}_{(-1)}a^{2},\mathfrak{p}_{2})(v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle S((u_{3}\cdot[a^{1}])\cdot[a^{2}],(v,\mathfrak{q})u_{2})z_{2}^{-\operatorname{wt}a^{2}-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}}

\displaystyle+\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}=\mathfrak{p}_{2}}\sum_{i\geqslant 0}F_{\operatorname{wt}a^{2}-1+\delta(s)+\frac{s}{T},i}(\mathfrak{p}_{2},\mathfrak{q})S\left\langle u_{3}\cdot[a^{1}]\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a^{2}_{(i)}v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle z_{1}^{-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}}(z_{1}-z_{2})^{-1}\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{z_{1}}

\displaystyle-\sum_{i\geqslant 0}\binom{\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-1+\delta(r)+\frac{r}{T}}{i+1}S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a^{1}_{(i)}a^{2},z_{2})(v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle z_{2}^{-i-1}

\displaystyle+\operatorname{Res}_{x_{1}}\frac{z_{2}^{-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}+1-\delta(r)-\frac{r}{T}}(w+x_{1})^{\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-1+\delta(r)+\frac{r}{T}}}{z_{2}-w-x_{1}}S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a^{2},\mathfrak{p}_{2})(Y_{M^{1}}(a^{1},x_{1})v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle-S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a^{1}_{(-1)}a^{2},\mathfrak{p}_{2})(v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\underbrace{S\left\langle(u_{3}\cdot[a^{1}])\cdot[a^{2}]\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle z_{2}^{-\operatorname{wt}a^{2}-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}}}_{(A1)}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\operatorname{Res}_{x_{2}}\frac{z_{2}^{-\operatorname{wt}a^{2}+1-\delta(s)-\frac{s}{T}-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}}(w+x_{2})^{\operatorname{wt}a^{2}-1+\delta(s)+\frac{s}{T}}}{z_{2}-w-x_{2}}S\left\langle u_{3}\cdot[a^{1}]\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(Y_{M^{1}}(a^{2},x_{2})v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle}_{(B1)}

\displaystyle\underbrace{-\sum_{i\geqslant 0}\binom{\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-1+\delta(r)+\frac{r}{T}}{i+1}S\left\langle u_{3}\cdot[a^{1}_{(i)}a^{2}]\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle z_{2}^{-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-\operatorname{wt}a^{2}}}_{(A2)}

\displaystyle\underbrace{-\sum_{i\geqslant 0}\binom{\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-1+\delta(r)+\frac{r}{T}}{i+1}\operatorname{Res}_{x_{2}}\frac{(w+x_{2})^{\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-i-1+\operatorname{wt}a^{2}-1+\delta(\overline{r+s})+\frac{\overline{r+s}}{T}}}{z_{2}-w-x_{2}}}_{(B_{2})}

\displaystyle\underbrace{\quad\cdot z_{2}^{-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-\operatorname{wt}a^{2}+1-\delta(\overline{r+s})-\frac{\overline{r+s}}{T}}S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(Y_{M^{1}}(a^{1}_{(i)}a^{2},x_{2})v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle}_{(B2)}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\operatorname{Res}_{x_{1}}\frac{z_{2}^{-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}+1-\delta(r)-\frac{r}{T}}(w+x_{1})^{\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-1+\delta(r)+\frac{r}{T}}}{z_{2}-w-x_{1}}S\left\langle u_{3}\cdot[a^{2}]\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(Y_{M^{1}}(a^{1},x_{1})v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle z_{2}^{-\operatorname{wt}a^{2}}}_{(B3)}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\operatorname{Res}_{x_{1}}\operatorname{Res}_{x_{2}}\frac{(w+x_{1})^{\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-1+\delta(r)+\frac{r}{T}}}{z_{2}-w-x_{1}}\cdot\frac{(w+x_{2})^{\operatorname{wt}a^{2}-1+\delta(s)+\frac{s}{T}}}{z_{2}-w-x_{2}}}_{(B4)}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}\cdot\underbrace{z_{2}^{-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-\operatorname{wt}a^{2}+2-\delta(r)-\delta(s)-\frac{s+r}{T}}S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(Y_{M^{1}}(a^{2},x_{2})Y_{M^{1}}(a^{1},x_{1})v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle}_{(B4)}

\displaystyle\underbrace{-S\left\langle u_{3}\cdot[a^{1}_{(-1)}a^{2}]\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle z_{2}^{-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-\operatorname{wt}a^{2}}}_{(A3)}

\displaystyle\underbrace{-\operatorname{Res}_{x_{2}}\frac{z_{2}^{-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-\operatorname{wt}a^{2}+1-\delta(\overline{r+s})-\frac{\overline{r+s}}{T}}(w+x_{2})^{\operatorname{wt}a^{1}+\operatorname{wt}a^{2}-1+\delta(\overline{r+s})+\frac{\overline{r+s}}{T}}}{z_{2}-w-x_{2}}}_{(B5)}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}\cdot\underbrace{S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(Y_{M^{1}}(a^{1}_{(-1)}a^{2},x_{2})v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle}_{(B5)}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle(A1)+(A2)+(A3)+(B1)+(B2)+(B3)+(B4)+(B5).

By
4.5
and
4.2
, we have

\displaystyle(A1)+(A2)+(A3)

\displaystyle=S\left\langle(u_{3}\cdot[a^{1}])\cdot[a^{2}]\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle z_{2}^{-\operatorname{wt}a^{2}-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}}

\displaystyle-\sum_{i\geqslant 0}\binom{\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-1+\delta(r)+\frac{r}{T}}{i+1}S\left\langle u_{3}\cdot[a^{1}_{(i)}a^{2}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle z_{2}^{-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-\operatorname{wt}a^{2}}

\displaystyle-S\left\langle u_{3}\cdot[a^{1}_{(-1)}a^{2}]\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle z_{2}^{-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-\operatorname{wt}a^{2}}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\left\langle\varphi\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(u_{3}\cdot[a^{1}])\cdot[a^{2}]\otimes v\otimes u_{2}-\sum_{j\geqslant 0}\binom{\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-1+\delta(r)+\frac{r}{T}}{j}u_{3}\cdot[a^{1}_{(j-1)}a^{2}]\otimes v\otimes u_{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\phantom{=}\cdot z_{2}^{\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-\operatorname{wt}a^{2}}w^{-\deg v}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle 0.

The last equality follows from the fact that
U^{3}
is a right module over
A_{g}(V)
. More precisely, if
r\neq 0
, we have
[a^{1}]=0
, and
\sum_{j\geqslant 0}\binom{\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-1+\frac{r}{T}}{j}[a^{1}_{(j-1)}a^{2}]=0
; if
r=0
and
s\neq 0
, the last equality holds since
[a^{2}]=[a^{1}_{(j-1)}a^{2}]=0
for all
j\geqslant 0
; if
r=s=0
, the last equality holds since
(u_{3}\cdot[a^{1}])\cdot[a^{2}]=u_{3}([a^{1}]\ast_{g}[a^{2}])
.

On the other hand, by the Jacobi identity, we can express
(B2)+(B5)
as follows:

\displaystyle(B2)+(B5)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle-\sum_{j\geqslant 0}\operatorname{Res}_{x_{2}}\operatorname{Res}_{x_{1}-x_{2}}(x_{1}-x_{2})^{j-1}\binom{\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-1+\delta(r)+\frac{r}{T}}{j}(x_{1}+x_{2})^{-j}

\displaystyle\cdot\frac{z_{2}^{-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-\operatorname{wt}a^{2}+1-\delta(\overline{r+s})-\frac{\overline{r+s}}{T}}(w+x_{2})^{\operatorname{wt}a^{1}+\operatorname{wt}a^{2}-1+\delta(\overline{r+s})+\frac{\overline{r+s}}{T}}}{z_{2}-w-x_{2}}

\displaystyle\cdot S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(Y_{M^{1}}(Y(a^{1},x_{1}-x_{2})a^{2},x_{2})v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle-\operatorname{Res}_{x_{2}}\operatorname{Res}_{x_{1}-x_{2}}\frac{1}{x_{1}-x_{2}}\left(1+\frac{x_{1}-x_{2}}{w+x_{2}}\right)^{\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-1+\delta(r)+\frac{r}{T}}z_{2}^{-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-\operatorname{wt}a^{2}+1-\delta(\overline{r+s})-\frac{\overline{r+s}}{T}}

\displaystyle\cdot\frac{(w+x_{2})^{\operatorname{wt}a^{1}+\operatorname{wt}a^{2}-1+\delta(\overline{r+s})+\frac{\overline{r+s}}{T}}}{z_{2}-w-x_{2}}S\langle u_{3}\,|\,\mathopen{}(Y_{M^{1}}(Y(a^{1},x_{1}-x_{2})a^{2},x_{2})v,\mathfrak{q})\,|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\rangle

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\underbrace{-\operatorname{Res}_{x_{1},x_{2}}\left(\frac{1}{x_{1}-x_{2}}\right)\frac{(w+x_{1})^{\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-1+\delta(r)+\frac{r}{T}}(w+x_{2})^{\operatorname{wt}a^{2}+\delta(\overline{r+s})+\frac{\overline{r+s}}{T}-\delta(r)-\frac{r}{T}}}{z_{2}-w-x_{2}}}_{(C1)}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}\underbrace{\cdot z_{2}^{-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-\operatorname{wt}a^{2}+1-\delta(\overline{r+s})-\frac{\overline{r+s}}{T}}S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(Y_{M^{1}}(a^{1},x_{1})Y_{M^{1}}(a^{2},x_{2})v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle}_{(C1)}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\operatorname{Res}_{x_{1},x_{2}}\left(\frac{1}{-x_{2}+x_{1}}\right)\frac{(w+x_{1})^{\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-1+\delta(r)+\frac{r}{T}}(w+x_{2})^{\operatorname{wt}a^{2}+\delta(\overline{r+s})+\frac{\overline{r+s}}{T}-\delta(r)-\frac{r}{T}}}{z_{2}-w-x_{2}}}_{(C2)}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}\underbrace{\cdot z_{2}^{-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-\operatorname{wt}a^{2}+1-\delta(\overline{r+s})-\frac{\overline{r+s}}{T}}S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(Y_{M^{1}}(a^{2},x_{2})Y_{M^{1}}(a^{1},x_{1})v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle}_{(C2)}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle(C1)+(C2).

By
Lemma

5.4
, we have

\displaystyle(C1)

\displaystyle=-\sum_{j\geqslant 0}\operatorname{Res}_{x_{2}}\frac{z_{2}^{-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-\operatorname{wt}a^{2}+1-\delta(\overline{r+s})-\frac{\overline{r+s}}{T}}(w+x_{2})^{\operatorname{wt}a^{2}+\delta(\overline{r+s})+\frac{\overline{r+s}}{T}-\delta(r)-\frac{r}{T}}x_{2}^{j}}{z_{2}-w-x_{2}}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}\cdot S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(\operatorname{Res}_{x_{1}}\frac{(w+x_{1})^{\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-1+\delta(r)+\frac{r}{T}}}{x_{1}^{j+1}}Y_{M^{1}}(a^{1},x_{1})Y_{M^{1}}(a^{2},x_{2})v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-\operatorname{Res}_{x_{2}}\frac{z_{2}^{-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-\operatorname{wt}a^{2}+1-\delta(\overline{r+s})-\frac{\overline{r+s}}{T}}(w+x_{2})^{\operatorname{wt}a^{2}+\delta(\overline{r+s})+\frac{\overline{r+s}}{T}-\delta(r)-\frac{r}{T}}x_{2}^{j}}{z_{2}-w-x_{2}}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}\cdot S\left\langle u_{3}\cdot[a^{1}]\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(Y_{M^{1}}(a^{2},x_{2})v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-(B1).

The last equality holds since both
(B1)
and
(C1)
are equal to
0
when
r\neq 0
.

For
(C2)+(B4)
, using
Lemma

5.4
again, we have

\displaystyle(C2)+(B4)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\operatorname{Res}_{x_{1},x_{2}}\left(\frac{1}{-x_{2}+x_{1}}\right)\frac{(w+x_{1})^{\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-1+\delta(r)+\frac{r}{T}}(w+x_{2})^{\operatorname{wt}a^{2}+\delta(\overline{r+s})+\frac{\overline{r+s}}{T}-\delta(r)-\frac{r}{T}}}{z_{2}-w-x_{2}}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}\cdot z_{2}^{-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-\operatorname{wt}a^{2}+1-\delta(\overline{r+s})-\frac{\overline{r+s}}{T}}S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(Y_{M^{1}}(a^{2},x_{2})Y_{M^{1}}(a^{1},x_{1})v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\operatorname{Res}_{x_{1}}\operatorname{Res}_{x_{2}}\frac{(w+x_{1})^{\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-1+\delta(r)+\frac{r}{T}}}{z_{2}-w-x_{1}}\cdot\frac{(w+x_{2})^{\operatorname{wt}a^{2}-1+\delta(s)+\frac{s}{T}}}{z_{2}-w-x_{2}}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}\cdot z_{2}^{-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-\operatorname{wt}a^{2}+2-\delta(r)-\delta(s)-\frac{r+s}{T}}S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(Y_{M^{1}}(a^{2},x_{2})Y_{M^{1}}(a^{1},x_{1})v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle.

Note that
(C2)+(B4)
varies when
r
and
s
take different values. There are 6 cases in total: (1)
r=s=0
, (2)
r=0
and
s\neq 0
, (3)
r\neq 0
and
s=0
, (4)
r+s=T
, (5)
r,s\neq 0
, and
r+s<T
, and (6)
r+s>T
. We only present the proof for the case
r=s=0
and the case
r+s=T
. The proof of other cases are similar, we omit the details.

Case (1):
r=s=0
. In this case,

\displaystyle(C2)+(B4)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\operatorname{Res}_{x_{1}}\operatorname{Res}_{x_{2}}\frac{(w+x_{1})^{\operatorname{wt}a^{1}}(w+x_{2})^{\operatorname{wt}a^{2}}}{z_{2}-w-x_{2}}\left(\frac{1}{-x_{2}+x_{1}}+\frac{1}{z_{2}-w-x_{1}}\right)z_{2}^{-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-\operatorname{wt}a^{2}}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}\cdot S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(Y_{M^{1}}(a^{2},x_{2})Y_{M^{1}}(a^{1},x_{1})v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\operatorname{Res}_{x_{1}}\operatorname{Res}_{x_{2}}\frac{(w+x_{1})^{\operatorname{wt}a^{1}}(w+x_{2})^{\operatorname{wt}a^{2}}}{z_{2}-w-x_{2}}\left(\frac{z_{2}-w-x_{2}}{(-x_{2}+x_{1})(z_{2}-w-x_{1})}\right)z_{2}^{-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-\operatorname{wt}a^{2}}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}\cdot S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(Y_{M^{1}}(a^{2},x_{2})Y_{M^{1}}(a^{1},x_{1})v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=-

\displaystyle\operatorname{Res}_{x_{1}}\frac{(w+x_{1})^{\operatorname{wt}a^{1}}}{z_{2}-w-x_{1}}\operatorname{Res}_{x_{2}}\sum_{j\geqslant 0}\left(\frac{(w+x_{2})^{\operatorname{wt}a^{2}}}{x_{2}^{1+j}}\right)x_{1}^{j}z_{2}^{-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-\operatorname{wt}a^{2}}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}\cdot S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(Y_{M^{1}}(a^{2},x_{2})Y_{M^{1}}(a^{1},x_{1})v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle-(B3),

where the last equality follows from
Lemma

5.4
.

Case (4):
r+s=T
. Since
r,s\neq 0
, we have

\displaystyle(C2)+(B4)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\operatorname{Res}_{x_{1}}\operatorname{Res}_{x_{2}}\frac{(w+x_{1})^{\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-1+\frac{r}{T}}(w+x_{2})^{\operatorname{wt}a^{2}+\frac{s}{T}}}{z_{2}-w-x_{2}}\left(\frac{1}{-x_{2}+x_{1}}\right)z_{2}^{-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-\operatorname{wt}a^{2}}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}\cdot S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(Y_{M^{1}}(a^{2},x_{2})Y_{M^{1}}(a^{1},x_{1})v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\operatorname{Res}_{x_{1}}\operatorname{Res}_{x_{2}}\frac{(w+x_{1})^{\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-1+\frac{r}{T}}(w+x_{2})^{\operatorname{wt}a^{2}-1+\frac{s}{T}}}{z_{2}-w-x_{2}}\left(\frac{1}{z_{2}-w-x_{1}}\right)z_{2}^{-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-\operatorname{wt}a^{2}+1}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}\cdot S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(Y_{M^{1}}(a^{2},x_{2})Y_{M^{1}}(a^{1},x_{1})v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\operatorname{Res}_{x_{1}}\operatorname{Res}_{x_{2}}\frac{(w+x_{1})^{\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-1+\frac{r}{T}}(w+x_{2})^{\operatorname{wt}a^{2}-1+\frac{s}{T}}}{z_{2}-w-x_{2}}\left(\frac{w+x_{2}}{-x_{2}+x_{1}}+\frac{z_{2}}{z_{2}-w-x_{1}}\right)

\displaystyle\phantom{=}\cdot z_{2}^{-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-\operatorname{wt}a^{2}}S\langle u_{3}\,|\,\mathopen{}(Y_{M^{1}}(a^{2},x_{2})Y_{M^{1}}(a^{1},x_{1})v,\mathfrak{q})\,|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\rangle

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\operatorname{Res}_{x_{1}}\operatorname{Res}_{x_{2}}\frac{(w+x_{1})^{\operatorname{wt}a^{1}+\frac{r}{T}}(w+x_{2})^{\operatorname{wt}a^{2}-1+\frac{s}{T}}}{z_{2}-w-x_{1}}\left(\frac{1}{-x_{2}+x_{1}}\right)z_{2}^{-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-\operatorname{wt}a^{2}}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}\cdot S\langle u_{3}\,|\,\mathopen{}(Y_{M^{1}}(a^{2},x_{2})Y_{M^{1}}(a^{1},x_{1})v,\mathfrak{q})\,|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\rangle

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle-\operatorname{Res}_{x_{1}}\frac{(w+x_{1})^{\operatorname{wt}a^{1}+\frac{r}{T}}}{z_{2}-w-x_{1}}\sum_{j\geqslant 0}\operatorname{Res}_{x_{2}}\left(\frac{(w+x_{2})^{\operatorname{wt}a^{2}-1+\frac{s}{T}}}{x_{2}^{1+j}}\right)x_{1}^{j}z_{2}^{-\operatorname{wt}a^{1}-\operatorname{wt}a^{2}}

\displaystyle\phantom{=}\cdot S\langle u_{3}\,|\,\mathopen{}(Y_{M^{1}}(a^{2},x_{2})Y_{M^{1}}(a^{1},x_{1})v,\mathfrak{q})\,|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\rangle

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle 0=-(B3).

Therefore, we have
(B1)+(B2)+(B3)+(B4)+(B5)=((B1)+(C1))+((C2)+(B4)+(B3))=0
, and so

\operatorname{Res}_{\mathfrak{p}_{1}=\mathfrak{p}_{2}}S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a^{1},\mathfrak{p}_{1})(a^{2},\mathfrak{p}_{2})(v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle(z_{1}-z_{2})^{-1}\mathinner{\mathrm{d}}\mathchoice{\mskip-3.0mu}{\mskip-3.0mu}{}{}{}{z_{1}}=S\left\langle u_{3}\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}(a^{1}_{(-1)}a^{2},\mathfrak{p}_{2})(v,\mathfrak{q})\,\middle|\,\mathopen{}u_{2}\right\rangle.

This proves
5.2
for
k=-1
. When
k<-1
, it can be proved inductively using the
L_{(-1)}
-derivative property
, we omit the details.
∎

Proposition 5.6
.

The system of
(n+3)
-point functions
S=\left\{S_{V\cdots M\cdots V}\right\}
constructed from the given
\varphi\in\operatorname{{C}}\left(U^{3},M^{1},U^{2}\right)
in
Section

4
lies in
\operatorname{Cor}\left(\Sigma_{1}(U^{3},M^{1},U^{2})\right)
.

Proof.

It follows from the construction of
S
,
Propositions

4.12
,
5.1
, and
5.5
.
∎

Now the proof of
Theorem

4.5
is complete.

6
Fusion rules characterized by
A_{g}(V)
-bimodules

In
Definition

5.2
, we constructed a quotient space
B_{g,\lambda}(M)=M/O_{g,\lambda}(M)
associated to an untwisted module
M
and a complex number
\lambda
.

In this section, we will show that
B_{g,\lambda}(M)=M/O_{g,\lambda}(M)
is in fact a bimodule over
A_{g}(V)
, and the space of coinvariants
(U^{3}\otimes M^{1}\otimes U^{2})/J
in
Definition

4.1
is isomorphic to the tensor product space
U^{3}\otimes_{A_{g}(V)}B_{g,\lambda}(M^{1})\otimes_{A_{g}(V)}U^{2}
, where
\lambda=h_{2}-h_{3}
. Moreover, we will show that the tensor products
U^{3}\otimes_{A_{g}(V)}B_{g,\lambda}(M^{1})\otimes_{A_{g}(V)}U^{2}
is isomorphic to the tensor product
U^{3}\otimes_{A_{g}(V)}A_{g}(M^{1})\otimes_{A_{g}(V)}U^{2}
, which gives us two ways to compute fusion rules using
A_{g}(V)
-bimodules. Finally, we will show that the
g
-twisted fusion rules are all finite when the VOA
V
is
g
-rational and
C_{2}
-cofinite.

6.1
The
A_{g}(V)
-bimodules
B_{g,\lambda}(M^{1})

Lemma 6.1
.

Let
M
be an untwisted module of conformal weight
h_{1}
, and let
\lambda
be a complex number. For
a\in V^{r}
and
u\in M
, define

\displaystyle a\ast_{g}u:=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\operatorname{Res}_{z}Y_{M}(a,z)u\frac{(1+z)^{\operatorname{wt}a}}{z}&\mathrm{if}\ r=0\\
0&\mathrm{if}\ r\neq 0\end{cases},

(6.1)

\displaystyle u\ast_{g}a:=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\operatorname{Res}_{z}Y_{M}(a,z)u\frac{(1+z)^{\operatorname{wt}a-1}}{z}&\mathrm{if}\ r=0\\
0&\mathrm{if}\ r\neq 0\end{cases}.

Then, we have
b\ast_{g}O_{g,\lambda}(M)\subseteq O_{g,\lambda}(M)
and
O_{g,\lambda}(M)\ast_{g}b\subseteq O_{g,\lambda}(M)
for any
b\in V
. Moreover,
B_{g,\lambda}(M)=M/O_{g,\lambda}(M)
is a bimodule over
A_{g}(V)
, with respect to the products
a\ast_{g}u
and
u\ast_{g}a
in
6.1
.

Proof.

In
Jiang and Jiao 2016
, Jiang and Jiao introduced a quotient space:

A_{g}(M)=M/O_{g}(M),

(6.2)

where
O_{g}(M)
is spanned by
a\circ_{g}u
for all
a\in V,u\in M
, and
a\circ_{g}u
is given by
5.5
. They proved that
A_{g}(M)
is an
A_{g}(V)
-bimodule with left and right actions given by
6.1
. In particular,
b\ast_{g}O_{g}(M)\subseteq O_{g}(M)
and
O_{g}(M)\ast_{g}b\subseteq O_{g}(M)
for all
b\in V
. Since
a\circ_{g}u=a\circ u
for
a\in V^{0}
, we introduce an intermediate subspace

O^{0}_{g,\lambda}(M):=\operatorname{span}\left\{a\circ_{g}u,L_{(-1)}u+(L_{(0)}+\lambda)u:a\in V^{0},u\in M\right\}\subseteq O_{g,\lambda}(M).

By
(
Liu 2023
, Lemmas 4.3, 4.4 and 4.5)
, together with the fact that
b\ast_{g}u=u\ast_{g}b=0
for
b\in V^{r}
with
r>0
, we have
b\ast_{g}O^{0}_{g,\lambda}(M)\subseteq O^{0}_{g,\lambda}(M)
and
O^{0}_{g,\lambda}(M)\ast_{g}b\subseteq O^{0}_{g,\lambda}(M)
for all
b\in V
. In particular, for any
u\in M
, we have

b\ast_{g}(L_{(-1)}u+(L_{(0)}+\lambda)u),\ (L_{(-1)}u+(L_{(0)}+\lambda)u)\ast_{g}b\in O^{0}_{g,\lambda}(M)\subseteq O_{g,\lambda}(M).

Then the conclusion follows from

O_{g,\lambda}(M)=O_{g}(M)+\operatorname{span}\left\{L_{(-1)}u+(L_{(0)}+\lambda)u:u\in M\right\},

in view of
5.6
.
By
Lemma

6.1
and
(
Jiang and Jiao 2016
, Theorem 3.4)
,
B_{g,\lambda}(M)=M/O_{g,\lambda}(M)
is a bimodule over
A_{g}(V)
with respect to the products
a\ast_{g}u
and
u\ast_{g}a
in
6.1
.
∎

Since there is an epimorphism of associative algebras
A(V^{0})\longrightarrow A_{g}(V)
(
Dong et al. 1998
),
B_{g,\lambda}(M)
and
A_{g}(M)
are also bimodules over
A(V^{0})
with actions
[a]\cdot[u]=[a\ast_{g}u]=[a\ast u]
and
[u]\cdot[a]=[u\ast_{g}a]=[u\ast a]
, where
a\in V^{0}
and
[u]\in B_{g,\lambda}(M)
or
A_{g}(M)
, in view of
6.1
.

Proposition 6.2
.

Let
M^{1}
be an untwisted module of conformal weight
h_{1}
,
U^{2}
(resp.
U^{3}
) be a left (resp. right) irreducible
A_{g}(V)
-modules on which
[\upomega]
acts as
h_{2}\operatorname{id}
(resp.
h_{3}\operatorname{id}
). Then we have an isomorphism of vector spaces

\displaystyle(U^{3}\otimes M^{1}\otimes U^{2})/J\cong U^{3}\otimes_{A_{g}(V)}B_{g,\lambda}(M^{1})\otimes_{A_{g}(V)}U^{2}\cong U^{3}\otimes_{A(V^{0})}B_{g,\lambda}(M^{1})\otimes_{A(V^{0})}U^{2},

(6.3)

where
J
is given by
4.1
–
4.4
, and
\lambda=h_{2}-h_{3}
.

Proof.

Define a linear map

\displaystyle\phi:U^{3}\otimes M^{1}\otimes U^{2}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow U^{3}\otimes_{A_{g}(V)}B_{g,\lambda}(M^{1})\otimes_{A_{g}(V)}U^{2},

\displaystyle\phi(u_{3}\otimes v\otimes u_{2}):

\displaystyle=u_{3}\otimes[v]\otimes u_{2},\quad u_{3}\in U^{3},v\in M^{1},u_{2}\in U^{2},

where
[v]
is the image of
v\in M^{1}
in
B_{g,\lambda}(M^{1})
. By
Definition

5.2
, it is straightforward to see that
\phi
factors through
(U^{3}\otimes M^{1}\otimes U^{2})/J
. Denote the induced map by
\overline{\phi}
.

Conversely, we consider the following linear map:

\psi:U^{3}\otimes_{A_{g}(V)}B_{g,\lambda}(M^{1})\otimes_{A_{g}(V)}U^{2}\longrightarrow(U^{3}\otimes CM^{1}\otimes CU^{2})/J,\ \psi(u_{3}\otimes[v]\otimes u_{2}):=u_{3}\otimes v\otimes u_{2}+J.

Indeed, by
Lemma

5.3
, we have
\psi(u_{3}\otimes[O_{g,\lambda}(M^{1})]\otimes u_{2})=u_{3}\otimes O_{g,\lambda}(M^{1})\otimes u_{2}+J=0
. Furthermore, recall that
A_{g}(V)
is a quotient of
A(V^{0})

Dong et al. 1998
, and so
U^{2}
and
U^{3}
are also left and right modules over
A(V^{0})
, respectively. Let
a\in V^{0}
, by
4.2
,
4.3
, and
6.1
, we have

\displaystyle\psi(u_{3}[a]\otimes[v]\otimes u_{2})=\psi(u_{3}\otimes[a\ast_{g}v]\otimes u_{2}),\ \psi(u_{3}\otimes[v]\otimes[a]u_{2})=\psi(u_{3}\otimes[v\ast_{g}a]\otimes u_{2}).

Hence
\psi
is well-defined. It is clear that
\psi
is an inverse of
\bar{\phi}
. Observe that
[a\ast_{g}v]=[a\ast v]
and
[v\ast_{g}a]=[v\ast a]
for
a\in V^{0}
, then by adopting a similar argument, we can also show that
(U^{3}\otimes CM^{1}\otimes CU^{2})/J\cong U^{3}\otimes_{A(V^{0})}B_{g,\lambda}(M^{1})\otimes_{A(V^{0})}U^{2}
.
∎

Although the
A_{g}(V)
-bimodules
B_{g,h_{2}-h_{3}}(M^{1})
and
A_{g}(M^{1})
are not isomorphic in general, the tensor products
U^{3}\otimes_{A_{g}(V)}B_{g,h_{2}-h_{3}}(M^{1})\otimes_{A_{g}(V)}U^{2}
and
U^{3}\otimes_{A_{g}(V)}A_{g}(M^{1})\otimes_{A_{g}(V)}U^{2}
are in fact isomorphic. This isomorphism was proved in
Liu 2023
for the untwisted case under the assumption that
A(V)
is semi-simple. Now we drop the semi-simplicity condition.

Proposition 6.3
.

With the aforementioned assumptions, we have a linear isomorphism:

\displaystyle U^{3}\otimes_{A_{g}(V)}B_{g,h_{2}-h_{3}}(M^{1})\otimes_{A_{g}(V)}U^{2}\cong U^{3}\otimes_{A_{g}(V)}A_{g}(M^{1})\otimes_{A_{g}(V)}U^{2}

(6.4)

\displaystyle\cong U^{3}\otimes_{A(V^{0})}B_{g,h_{2}-h_{3}}(M^{1})\otimes_{A(V^{0})}U^{2}\cong U^{3}\otimes_{A(V^{0})}A_{g}(M^{1})\otimes_{A(V^{0})}U^{2}.

Proof.

By
5.6
and
Lemma

6.1
,
B_{g,h_{2}-h_{3}}(M^{1})=A_{g}(M^{1})/I
, where

I=\operatorname{span}\left\{[(L_{(-1)}+L_{(0)}+h_{2}-h_{3})u]\nonscript\>\middle|\allowbreak\nonscript\>\mathopen{}u\in M^{1}\right\}=O_{g,h_{2}-h_{3}}(M^{1})/O_{g}(M^{1})

is a sub-bimodule of
A_{g}(M^{1})
.

Observe that

\upomega\ast_{g}u-u\ast_{g}\upomega+(h_{2}-h_{3})u=(L_{(-1)}+L_{(0)}+h_{2}-h_{3})u
for any
u\in M^{1}
, where
\upomega\in V
is the conformal vector. Thus,

I=\operatorname{span}\left\{[\upomega]\ast_{g}[u]-[u]\ast_{g}[\upomega]+(h_{2}-h_{3})[u]\nonscript\>\middle|\allowbreak\nonscript\>\mathopen{}u\in M^{1}\right\}\subseteq A_{g}(M^{1}).

(6.5)

Recall that
[\upomega]\in A_{g}(V)
is a central element
Dong et al. 1998
;
Zhu 1996
,
I
is a sub-bimodule of
A_{g}(M^{1})
. (This gives an alternative proof of
Lemma

6.1
.) Denote the inclusion map
I\lhook\joinrel\rightarrow A_{g}(M^{1})
by
\iota
, and
A_{g}(V)
by
A
for short, then by the right exactness of tensor functor, we have a right exact sequence:

U^{3}\otimes_{A}I\otimes_{A}U^{2}\overset{1\otimes\iota\otimes 1}{\longrightarrow}U^{3}\otimes_{A}A_{g}(M^{1})\otimes_{A}U^{2}\to U^{3}\otimes_{A}(A_{g}(M^{1})/I)\otimes_{A}U^{2}\to 0.

(6.6)

We claim that
(1\otimes\iota\otimes 1)(U^{3}\otimes_{A}I\otimes_{A}U^{2})=0
in
U^{3}\otimes_{A}A_{g}(M^{1})\otimes_{A}U^{2}
. Indeed, for any
u_{3}\in U^{3}
,
u_{2}\in U^{2}
, and
u\in M^{1}
, we have

\displaystyle(1\otimes\iota\otimes 1)(u_{3}\otimes([\upomega]\ast[u]-[u]\ast[\upomega]+(h_{2}-h_{3})[u])\otimes u_{2})

\displaystyle=u_{3}([\upomega]-h_{3})\otimes[u]\otimes v_{2}-u_{3}\otimes[u]\otimes([\upomega]-h_{2})u_{2}

\displaystyle=0.

in
U^{3}\otimes_{A}A_{g}(M^{1})\otimes_{A}U^{2}
. Then, the first isomorphism in
6.4
follows from
6.6
. The last two isomorphism follows from
Proposition

6.2
and its proof.
∎

Remark 6.4
.

In the last remark of
Liu 2023
, the author made a false claim that the isomorphism
6.4
does not hold in general for the untwisted case. This was due to a mistake in Example 4.22 in
Liu 2023
. We make a correction here:

In the isomorphism
B_{h}(M(c,h_{1}))\otimes_{A(M_{c})}M_{c}(0)\cong\mathbb C[t_{0}]\otimes_{\mathbb C[t]}M_{c}(0)\cong M_{c}(0)
, the left
A(M(c,0))=\mathbb C[t]
-action is given by

\displaystyle t.(1\otimes v_{c,0})=(t_{0}+h_{2})\otimes v_{c,0}=1.t\otimes v_{c,0}+h_{2}\otimes v_{c,0}=1\otimes L_{(0)}v_{c,0}+h_{2}\otimes v_{c,0}=h_{2}\otimes v_{c,0}.

Thus the left
\mathbb C[t]
-module
\mathbb C[t_{0}]\otimes_{\mathbb C[t]}M_{c}(0)\cong M_{c}(0)
is isomorphic to
M(c,h_{2})(0)=\mathbb Cv_{c,h_{2}}
, and so

((M(c,h_{2})(0))^{\ast}\otimes_{A(M_{c})}B_{h}(M(c,h_{1}))\otimes_{A(M_{c})}M_{c}(0))^{\ast}\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{A(M_{c})}(M_{c}(0),M(c,h_{2})(0))
is
1
-dimensional, same as
(M(c,h_{2})(0)^{\ast}\otimes_{A(M_{c})}A(M(c,h_{1}))\otimes_{A(M_{c})}M_{c}(0))^{\ast}
.

Theorem 6.5
.

Let
M^{1}
be an untwisted lowest weight module of conformal weight
h_{1}
,
M^{2}
(resp.
M^{3}
) be a lowest weight
g
-twisted module of conformal weight
h_{2}
(resp.
h_{3}
) with bottom level
U_{2}
(resp.
U_{3}
). Further, assume
M^{2}
and
(M^{3})^{\prime}
are generalized Verma module. Then, we have isomorphisms:

\mathfrak{I}\textstyle\binom{M^{3}}{M^{1}\;M^{2}}\cong\operatorname{Cor}\left(\Sigma_{1}((U^{3})^{\ast},M^{1},U^{2})\right)\cong((U^{3})^{\ast}\otimes_{A_{g}(V)}A_{g}(M^{1})\otimes_{A_{g}(V)}U^{2})^{\ast}.

(6.7)

In particular, if
V
is
g
-rational, then
6.7
holds for any untwisted irreducible module
M^{1}
, and irreducible
g
-twisted modules
M^{2}
and
M^{3}
.

Proof.

It follows from
Theorems

2.24
,
4.5
,
3.17
, and
6.3
.
∎

In general, we have the following upper bound of the
g
-twisted fusion rules by
Proposition

3.8
:

N\textstyle\binom{M^{3}}{M^{1}\;M^{2}}\leqslant\dim\left((M^{3}(0))^{\ast}\otimes_{A_{g}(V)}A_{g}(M^{1})\otimes_{A_{g}(V)}M^{2}(0)\right)^{\ast},

(6.8)

where
M^{2}
and
M^{3}
are irreducible
g
-twisted modules with bottom levels
M^{2}(0)
and
M^{3}(0)
, respectively, and
V
is an arbitrary VOA.

6.2
The finiteness of twisted conformal blocks and fusion rules

In this subsection, we assume that
V
is of CFT-type, i.e.
V=V_{0}\oplus V_{+}
, with
V_{0}=\mathbb C\mathbb{1}
, and
V_{+}=\bigoplus_{n=1}^{\infty}V_{n}
.

The finiteness of fusion rules is one of the standard assumptions for rational conformal field theory
Moore and Seiberg 1989
. Li proved the finiteness of fusion rules among three irreducible untwisted modules
M^{1},M^{2}
, and
M^{3}
when the module
M^{1}
is
C_{2}
-cofinite
Li 1999
. Using
Theorem

6.5
and
6.8
, we can show the finiteness of
g
-twisted fusion rules under the same condition.

We observe that the filtrations on
A(V)
and
A(M)
studied in
Liu 2023
also have a
g
-twisted analog. Define a filtration:
0=A_{g}(V)_{-1}\subseteq A_{g}(V)_{0}\subseteq A_{g}(V)_{1}\subseteq\cdots
on
A_{g}(V)
by

A_{g}(V)_{n}:=\left(\bigoplus_{i=0}^{n}V_{i}+O_{g}(V)\right)/O_{g}(V),\quad n\in\mathbb Z.

(6.9)

It is clear that
A_{g}(V)_{m}\ast_{g}A_{g}(V)_{n}\subseteq A_{g}(V)_{m+n}
for any
m,n\in\mathbb N
. Denote the associated graded algebra by
\operatorname{gr}A_{g}(V)=\bigoplus_{n=0}^{\infty}(A_{g}(V)_{n}/A_{g}(V)_{n-1})
. The product
\cdot\ast_{g}\cdot
on
\operatorname{gr}A_{g}(V)
is given by

([a]+A_{g}(V)_{m-1})\ast_{g}([b]+A_{g}(V)_{n-1}):=[a]\ast_{g}[b]+A_{g}(V)_{m+n-1},

(6.10)

where
[a]\in A_{g}(V)_{m}
and
[b]\in A_{g}(V)_{n}
. It is easy to check this product is commutative.

Recall that
R(V)=V/C_{2}(V)
is a commutative associative algebra with product
(a+C_{2}(V))\cdot(b+C_{2}(V))=a_{(-1)}b+C_{2}(V)
, see
Zhu 1996
. The following Proposition is a
g
-twisted version of
(
Liu 2023
, Theorem 2.6)
and a refinement of
(
Dong et al. 2000
, Proposition 3.6)
:

Proposition 6.6
.

The linear map
\phi:V\longrightarrow\operatorname{gr}A_{g}(V)
,
\phi(a)=[a]+A_{g}(V)_{m-1}
for
a\in\oplus_{i=0}^{m}V_{i}
, factors through
C_{2}(V)
. It induces an epimorphism of commutative associative algebras:

\phi:R(V)=V/C_{2}(V)\longrightarrow\operatorname{gr}A_{g}(V),\quad\phi(a+C_{2}(V))=[a]+A_{g}(V)_{m},\ \mathrm{where}\ a\in\oplus_{i=0}^{m}V_{i}.

(6.11)

Proof.

First we show that
\phi(C_{2}(V))=0
. Let
a\in V^{r}_{m}
and
b\in V_{n}
. Since
\operatorname{wt}(a_{(-2)}b)=m+n+1
, we have
\phi(a_{(-2)}b)=[a_{(-2)}b]+A_{g}(V)_{m+n}
. By Lemma 2.2 in
Dong et al. 1998
, we have

\operatorname{Res}_{z}Y(a,z)b\frac{(1+z)^{m-1+\delta(r)+\frac{r}{T}}}{z^{1+\delta(r)+k}}\in O_{g}(V),\quad k\geqslant 0.

(6.12)

We may choose
k=0
if
\delta(r)=1
, and
k=1
if
\delta(r)=0
. Then,

[a_{(-2)}b]+A_{g}(V)_{m+n}=-\sum_{j\geqslant 0}\binom{m-1+\delta(r)+\frac{r}{T}}{j+1}[a_{(j-1)}b]+A_{g}(V)_{m+n}=[0]+A_{g}(V)_{m+n}

since
\operatorname{wt}a_{(j-1)}b=m+n-j\leqslant m+n
for any
j\geqslant 0
. Hence
\phi
in
6.11
is well-defined.

Moreover,
\phi((a+C_{2}(v))\cdot(b+C_{2}(V)))=\phi(a_{(-1)}b+C_{2}(V))=[a_{(-1)}b]+A_{g}(V)_{m+n-1}
. If
0<r<T
, by
6.12
with
k=0
,
3.1
and
6.10
, we have

\displaystyle[a_{(-1)}b]+A_{g}(V)_{m+n-1}

\displaystyle=-\sum_{j\geqslant 0}\binom{m-1+\frac{r}{T}}{j+1}[a_{(j)}b]+A_{g}(V)_{m+n-1}=[0]+A_{g}(V)_{m+n-1}

\displaystyle=[a]\ast_{g}[b]+A_{g}(V)_{m+n-1}=\phi(a+C_{2}(V))\ast_{g}\phi(b+C_{2}(V))

since
\operatorname{wt}a_{(j)}b=m+n-j-1\leqslant m+n-1
for any
j\geqslant 0
. Finally, if
r=0
, by
3.1
again,

\displaystyle[a]\ast_{g}[b]+A_{g}(V)_{m+n-1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\geqslant 0}\binom{m}{j}[a_{(j-1)}b]+A_{g}(V)_{m+n-1}=[a_{(-1)}b]+A_{g}(V)_{m+n-1}.

Thus,
\phi
is an epimorphism of graded commutative associative algebras. ∎

Recall that a CFT-type VOA
V
is called
C_{1}
-cofinite, if
\dim V/C_{1}(V)<\infty
, where
C_{1}(V)=\operatorname{span}\left\{L_{(-1)}a:a\in V\right\}\cup\left\{a_{(-1)}b\nonscript\>\middle|\allowbreak\nonscript\>\mathopen{}a,b\in V_{+}\right\}
. Karel and Li proved in
Karel and Li 1999
that
R(V)
is finitely generated when
V
is
C_{1}
-cofinite.
As an immediate Corollary, we have the following fact about
A_{g}(V)
:

Corollary 6.7
.

A_{g}(V)
is an noetherian algebra if
V
is
C_{1}
-cofinite.

Let
M=\bigoplus_{n=0}^{\infty}M_{\lambda+n}
be an ordinary untwisted module of conformal weight
\lambda
. We introduce a similar filtration
0=A_{g}(M)_{-1}\subseteq A_{g}(M)_{0}\subseteq A_{g}(M)_{1}\subseteq\cdots
by

A_{g}(M)_{n}:=\left(\bigoplus_{i=0}^{n}M_{\lambda+i}+O_{g}(M)\right)/O_{g}(M),\quad n\in\mathbb Z.

It is clear that
A_{g}(M)
becomes a filtered
A_{g}(V)
-bimodule with this filtration and filtration
6.9
of
A_{g}(V)
, and
\operatorname{gr}A_{g}(M)
is a graded
\operatorname{gr}A_{g}(V)
-module. The following fact is similar to
Proposition

6.6
, and the proof is also similar:

Proposition 6.8
.

Let
M
be an untwisted module. There exists a epimomorphism of
R(V)
-modules

\psi:M/C_{2}(M)\longrightarrow\operatorname{gr}A_{g}(M),\quad\psi(u+C_{2}(M))=[u]+A_{g}(M)_{m},\ \mathrm{where}\ u\in\oplus_{i=0}^{m}M_{\lambda+i}.

(6.13)

Corollary 6.9
.

If
V
is
C_{2}
-cofinite, the
the twisted fusion rules among irreducible untwisted module
M^{1}
, and
g
-twisted modules
M^{2}
and
M^{3}
are finite.

Proof.

It was proved by Buhl in
Buhl 2002
that an irreducible untwisted module
M
is
C_{2}
-cofinite if
V
is
C_{2}
-cofinite. In particular, we have
\dim A_{g}(M^{1})=\dim\operatorname{gr}A_{g}(M^{1})<\infty
in view of
6.13
. Then, the conclusion follows from the estimate
6.8
.
∎

6.3
Twisted fusion rules and fusion rules for cyclic orbifold VOAs

Let
V
be a strongly rational VOA, and
g\in\operatorname{Aut}(V)
be an automorphism of finite order
T
. According to
Miyamoto 2015
;
Carnahan and Miyamoto 2016
, the cyclic orbifold subVOA
V^{0}=V^{\langle g\rangle}
is also strongly rational.

We again let
M^{1}
be an irreducible untwisted module, and
M^{2}
and
M^{3}
be irreducible
g
-twisted modules. Note that
M^{1}
,
M^{2}
, and
M^{3}
are also ordinary
V^{0}
-modules.
With
Theorem

6.5
and
(
Li 1999
, Theorem 2.11)
or
(
Liu 2023
, Theorem 4.19)
, we can find a concrete relation between the space of twisted intertwining operators
\mathfrak{I}\textstyle\binom{M^{3}}{M^{1}\;M^{2}}
, and the space of ordinary intertwining operators of
V^{0}
-modules, which we denote by
\mathfrak{I}_{V^{0}}\textstyle\binom{M^{3}}{M^{1}\;M^{2}}
.

By Theorem 3.1 in
Dong et al. 2017
, the twisted modules
M^{1},M^{2}
, and
M^{3}
decompose into direct sum of irreducible ordinary
V^{0}
-modules:

M^{1}=\bigoplus_{i=0}^{m_{1}}M^{1,i},

M^{2}=\bigoplus_{j=0}^{m_{2}}M^{2,j},
and
M^{3}=\bigoplus_{k=0}^{m_{3}}M^{3,k},

where the direct summands could appear multiple times. Denote the
A(V^{0})
-bimodule
M^{1,i}/O_{V^{0}}(M^{1,i})
by
A_{V^{0}}(M^{1,i})
for all
i
. Since
a\circ M^{1,i}\subseteq M^{1,i}
for all
i
and
a\in V^{0}
, we have

A_{V^{0}}(M^{1})=M^{1}/O_{V^{0}}(M^{1})\cong\bigoplus_{i=0}^{m_{1}}M^{1,i}/O_{V^{0}}(M^{1,i})=\bigoplus_{i=0}^{m_{1}}A_{V^{0}}(M^{1,i}).

Moreover, since
V^{0}
is also
C_{2}
-cofinite
Miyamoto 2015
, we have
\dim\mathfrak{I}_{V^{0}}\textstyle\binom{M^{3,k}}{M^{1,i}\;M^{2,j}}<\infty
for all
i,j,
and
k
. By taking restrictions and projections onto the direct summands, using
(
Liu 2023
, Theorem 4.19)
and the fact that
V^{0}
is rational
Carnahan and Miyamoto 2016
, we have the following identification of
\mathfrak{I}_{V^{0}}\textstyle\binom{M^{3}}{M^{1}\;M^{2}}
:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{I}_{V^{0}}\textstyle\binom{M^{3}}{M^{1}\;M^{2}}

\displaystyle=\bigoplus_{i,j,k}\mathfrak{I}_{V^{0}}\textstyle\binom{M^{3,k}}{M^{1,i}\;M^{2,j}}\cong\left(\bigoplus_{i,j,k}M^{3,k}(0)^{\ast}\otimes_{A(V^{0})}A_{V^{0}}(M^{1,i})\otimes_{A(V^{0})}M^{2,j}(0)\right)^{\ast}

\displaystyle\cong(M^{3}(0)^{\ast}\otimes_{A(V^{0})}M^{1}/O_{V^{0}}(M^{1})\otimes_{A(V^{0})}M^{2}(0))^{\ast}.

On the other hand, we have
\mathfrak{I}\textstyle\binom{M^{3}}{M^{1}\;M^{2}}\cong(M^{3}(0)^{\ast}\otimes_{A(V^{0})}M^{1}/O_{g}(M^{1})\otimes_{A(V^{0})}M^{2}(0))^{\ast},
in view of
Theorem

6.5
and
6.4
. By the proof of
Lemma

6.1
,
O_{g}(M^{1})/O_{V^{0}}(M^{1})
is an
A(V^{0})
-sub-bimodule of
A_{g}(M^{1})=M^{1}/O_{g}(M^{1})
. Thus, we have the following

Proposition 6.10
.

With the settings as above, we have the following relation between the fusion rules of
g
-twisted modules and fusion rules of ordinary
V^{0}
-modules:

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j,k}N\textstyle\binom{M^{3,k}}{M^{1,i}\;M^{2,j}}

(6.14)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle N\textstyle\binom{M^{3}}{M^{1}\;M^{2}}+\dim(M^{3}(0)^{\ast}\otimes_{A(V^{0})}\frac{O_{g}(M^{1})}{O_{V^{0}}(M^{1})}\otimes_{A(V^{0})}M^{2}(0)).

7
Fusion rules among
\theta
-twisted modules over the Heisenberg and lattice VOAs

In this Section, we apply
Theorem

6.5
to compute the fusion rules among
\theta
-twisted modules over the Heisenberg VOA and rank one lattice VOA. We refer to
Frenkel et al. 1988
;
Dong 1993
;
Dong and Lepowsky 1993
for the detailed constructions of the Heisenberg VOAs and lattice VOAs.

Here we recall the definition of involution
\theta
in
Frenkel et al. 1988
. Let
L
be a positive definite even lattice of rank
d>0
, and
\theta:L\longrightarrow L
be an involution of
L
defined by
\theta(\alpha)=-\alpha
, for any
\alpha\in L
. Then,
\theta
lifts to an involution of the Heisenberg VOA
M(1)
associated to
\mathfrak{h}=\mathbb C\otimes_{\mathbb Z}L
and the lattice VOA
V_{L}=M(1)\otimes\mathbb C^{\epsilon}[L]
as follows:

\displaystyle\theta:M(1)\longrightarrow M(1),\quad\theta(\alpha^{1}(-n_{1})\cdots\alpha^{k}(-n_{k})\mathbb{1}):=(-1)^{k}\alpha^{1}(-n_{1})\cdots\alpha^{k}(-n_{k})\mathbb{1},

(7.1)

\displaystyle\theta:V_{L}\longrightarrow V_{L},\quad\theta(\alpha^{1}(-n_{1})\cdots\alpha^{k}(-n_{k})e^{\alpha}):=(-1)^{k}\alpha^{1}(-n_{1})\cdots\alpha^{k}(-n_{k})e^{-\alpha},

(7.2)

where
\alpha^{1},\cdots,\alpha^{k}\in\mathfrak{h}
,
n_{1},\cdots,n_{k}\geqslant 1
, and
\alpha\in L
. Clearly,
\theta^{2}=1
. The
\theta
-eigenspaces of eigenvalue
1
in
M(1)
and
V_{L}
are denoted by
M(1)^{+}
and
V_{L}^{+}
, respectively, while the
-1
-eigenspaces are denoted by
M(1)^{-}
and
V_{L}^{-}
.

Since
M(1)
and
V_{L}
are both simple VOAs, by
(
Xu 1995
, Theorem 3.4)
, the only possible nonzero intertwining operators among
\theta
-twisted modules are of the type
\textstyle\binom{\theta}{1\;\theta}
,
\textstyle\binom{\theta}{\theta\;1}
, or
\textstyle\binom{1}{\theta\;\theta}
. Here type
\textstyle\binom{g_{3}}{g_{1}\;g_{2}}
means type
\textstyle\binom{M^{3}}{M^{1}\;M^{2}}
, where
M^{i}
is a
g_{i}
-twisted module for
i=1,2,3
. On the other hand, by
(
Huang 2018
, Corollary 5.2 and Corollary 6.2)
, we have

\mathfrak{I}\textstyle\binom{1}{\theta\;\theta}\cong\mathfrak{I}\textstyle\binom{\theta^{-1}}{\theta\;1}\cong\mathfrak{I}\textstyle\binom{\theta}{1\;\theta}.

Therefore, in order to determine the fusion rules among
\theta
-twisted modules, where
V=M(1)
or
V_{L}
, we only need to determine the space of
\mathfrak{I}\textstyle\binom{\theta}{1\;\theta}
-twisted intertwining operators.

7.1
The Heisenberg VOA case

Let
\mathfrak{h}=\mathbb C\otimes ZL
or any
d
-dimensional
\mathbb C
-vector space, equipped with a non-degenerate bilinear form
(\cdot|\cdot)
. Recall the twisted affine algebra
\hat{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathbb Z+\frac{1}{2}}=\mathfrak{h}\otimes t^{1/2}\mathbb C[t,t^{-1}]\oplus\mathbb CK
, with Lie bracket given by

[a(m),b(n)]=\delta_{m+n,0}m(a|b)K,\quad[K,\hat{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathbb Z+\frac{1}{2}}]=0,\quad a,b\in\mathfrak{h},m,n\in\mathbb Z+\frac{1}{2}.

Let
M(1)_{\mathbb Z+\frac{1}{2}}
be the induced module
U(\hat{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathbb Z+\frac{1}{2}})\otimes_{U(\hat{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathbb Z+1/2}^{+}\oplus\mathbb CK)}\mathbb C\mathbb{1}
, where
\hat{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathbb Z+1/2}^{+}\oplus\mathbb CK
acts trivially on
\mathbb C\mathbb{1}

Frenkel et al. 1988
;
Dong and Nagatomo 1999
. By Corollary 3.9 in
Dong and Nagatomo 1999
, the
\theta
-twisted Zhu’s algebra
A_{\theta}(M(1))
is isomorphic to
\mathbb C
, and
M(1)_{\mathbb Z+\frac{1}{2}}
is the unique
\theta
-twisted
M(1)
-module.

It is clear that
M(1)_{\mathbb Z+\frac{1}{2}}
is an irreducible module over twisted affine Lie algebra
\hat{\mathfrak{h}}_{\mathbb Z+\frac{1}{2}}
. Since
M(1)_{\mathbb Z+\frac{1}{2}}
is universal in the sense that any
\theta
-twisted
M(1)
-module with bottom level
\mathbb C\mathbb{1}
is a quotient module of
M(1)_{\mathbb Z+\frac{1}{2}}
, it is also the
\theta
-twisted generalized Verma module over
M(1)
.

Lemma 7.1
.

Let
M(1,\lambda)=M(1)\otimes\mathbb Ce^{\lambda}
be the irreducible (untwisted) module over
M(1)
associated to
\lambda\in\mathfrak{h}
. Then,
A_{\theta}(M(1,\lambda))=\mathbb C[e^{\lambda}]
.

Proof.

The proof is similar to the proof of Lemma 3.7 and 3.8 in
Dong and Nagatomo 1999
. We briefly sketch it. For any
\alpha\in\mathfrak{h}
, since
\alpha(-1)\mathbb{1}\in M(1)^{-}
, by
5.6
, we have

\alpha(-m-1)v\equiv-\sum_{j\geqslant 0}\binom{1/2}{j+1}\alpha(j-m)v\pmod{O_{\theta}(M(1,\lambda))},

(7.3)

for any
m\geqslant 0
,
v\in M(1,\lambda)
. We use induction on the degree
n_{1}+\cdots+n_{k}=n
of a spanning element
v=\alpha^{1}(-n_{1})\cdots\alpha^{k}(-n_{k})e^{\lambda}
of
M(1,\lambda)
to show that
v\equiv ce^{\lambda}\pmod{O_{\theta}}(M(1,\lambda))
, where
c\in\mathbb C
. The base case
n=0
is clear. For
n>0
, by
7.3
we have

\displaystyle\alpha^{1}(-n_{1})\cdots\alpha^{k}(-n_{k})e^{\lambda}

\displaystyle\equiv-\sum_{j\geqslant 0}\binom{1/2}{j+1}\alpha^{1}(j-n_{1}+1)\alpha^{2}(-n_{2})\cdots\alpha^{k}(-n_{k})e^{\lambda}\pmod{O_{\theta}(M(1,\lambda))},

where
\deg(\alpha^{1}(j-n_{1}+1)\alpha^{2}(-n_{2})\cdots\alpha^{k}(-n_{k})e^{\lambda})=n_{1}-j-1+n_{2}+\cdots+n_{k}=n-j-1<n
. Then, by the induction hypothesis,
v\equiv ce^{\lambda}\pmod{O_{\theta}(M(1,\lambda))}
.
∎

We note that
Lemma

7.1
only shows
A_{\theta}(M(1,\lambda))
is at most one-dimensional. It is not obvious that
[e^{\lambda}]\neq 0
. Using
Lemma

7.1
,
Theorem

6.5
, the Hom-tensor duality, and the fact
A_{\theta}(M(1))\cong\mathbb C
, we have

N\textstyle\binom{M_{\mathbb Z+\frac{1}{2}}(1)}{M(1,\lambda)\;M_{\mathbb Z+\frac{1}{2}}(1)}=\dim\operatorname{Hom}_{\mathbb C}(\mathbb C[e^{\lambda}]\otimes\mathbb C\mathbb{1},\mathbb C\mathbb{1})\leqslant 1.

(7.4)

On the other hand, Abe, Dong, and Li constructed a nonzero
\textstyle\binom{\theta}{1\;\theta}
-twisted intertwining operator in
Abe et al. 2005
based on twisted vertex operators from
Frenkel et al. 1988
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda}^{\mathrm{tw}}(\cdot,w):M(1,\lambda)\longrightarrow\operatorname{End}(M_{\mathbb Z+\frac{1}{2}}(1))\{w\},

(7.5)

\displaystyle\mathcal{Y}_{\lambda}^{\mathrm{tw}}(e^{\lambda},w)=e^{-|\lambda|^{2}\log 2}z^{-\frac{|\lambda|^{2}}{2}}\exp(\sum_{n\in-\frac{1}{2}-\mathbb N}\frac{-\lambda(n)}{n}z^{-n})\exp(\sum_{n\in\frac{1}{2}+\mathbb N}\frac{-\lambda(n)}{n}z^{-n}),

see equation (4.7) in
Abe et al. 2005
.
Using
7.4
, we have the following result about the fusion rules among
\theta
-twisted module over
M(1)
:

Proposition 7.2
.

Let
\lambda\in\mathfrak{h}^{\ast}
. Then,
N\textstyle\binom{M_{\mathbb Z+\frac{1}{2}}(1)}{M(1,\lambda)\;M_{\mathbb Z+\frac{1}{2}}(1)}=1
.

7.2
The rank one lattice VOA case

In this subsection, we assume that
L=\mathbb Z\alpha
, with
(\alpha|\alpha)=2
and
\epsilon(\alpha,\alpha)=1
. i.e.,
\epsilon:L\times L\longrightarrow<\pm 1>
is trivial. Then,
L
is the root lattice of type
A_{1}
, with dual lattice
L^{\circ}=\frac{1}{2}L=L\sqcup(L+\frac{1}{2}\alpha)
. We first recall some general results about twisted representations of
V_{L}
in
Frenkel et al. 1988
;
Dong 1993
;
Dong 1994
;
Dong and Nagatomo 1999
.

By
(
Dong 1993
, Theorem 3.1)
,
V_{L}
has two untwisted irreducible modules
V_{L}
and
V_{L+\frac{1}{2}\alpha}
. On the other hand, according to
(
Frenkel et al. 1988
, Theorem 3.5.1, Remark 3.5.3, and Remark 7.4.14)
, together with
(
Dong 1994
, Theorem 3.1)
,
V_{L}
has two irreducible
\theta
-twisted modules
V_{L}^{T_{\chi}}
and
V_{L}^{T_{-\chi}}
, with bottom levels
T_{\chi}=\mathbb Cv_{\chi}
and
T_{-\chi}=\mathbb Cv_{-\chi}
, respectively, where
\chi\in\mathbb C^{\times}
, and

V_{L}^{T_{\chi}}=M_{\mathbb Z+\frac{1}{2}}(1)\otimes\mathbb Cv_{\chi},\quad V_{L}^{T_{-\chi}}=M_{\mathbb Z+\frac{1}{2}}(1)\otimes\mathbb Cv_{-\chi}.

Moreover, consider the Lie algebra
sl_{2}=\mathbb Ce^{\alpha}+\mathbb C\alpha(-1)\mathbb{1}+\mathbb Ce^{-\alpha}=(V_{L})_{1}
, let
E=e^{\alpha}+e^{-\alpha}
and
F=e^{\alpha}-e^{-\alpha}
, and let

\hat{sl_{2}}[\theta_{2}]=(\mathbb CE\otimes\mathbb C[t,t^{-1}])\oplus((\mathbb C\alpha(-1)\mathbb{1}+\mathbb CF)\otimes t^{1/2}\mathbb C[t,t^{-1}])\oplus\mathbb CK

be the twisted affine Lie algebra associated to
sl_{2}
and the involution

\theta_{2}:sl_{2}\longrightarrow sl_{2},\quad e^{\alpha}\mapsto e^{-\alpha},\quad\alpha(-1)\mathbb{1}\mapsto-\alpha(-1)\mathbb{1},\quad e^{-\alpha}\mapsto e^{\alpha},

which is
\theta
in
7.2
restricted to
(V_{L})_{1}
. See Chapters 2 and 3 of
Frenkel et al. 1988
for more details. Then,
V_{L}^{T_{\chi}}
and
V_{L}^{T_{-\chi}}
are non-isomorphic irreducible modules over the twisted affine Lie algebra
\hat{sl_{2}}[\theta_{2}]
. Let
x(n):=x\otimes t^{n}\in\hat{sl_{2}}[\theta_{2}]
, for any
x\in sl_{2}
and
n\in\mathbb Z
or
\mathbb Z+\frac{1}{2}
. By the construction of twisted modules, the action of
E_{(0)}
on
T_{\chi}
and
T_{-\chi}
are given by

E_{(0)}v_{\chi}=\frac{1}{2}v_{\chi},\quad E_{(0)}v_{-\chi}=-\frac{1}{2}v_{-\chi}.

(7.6)

See Section 5.1 in
Dong and Nagatomo 1999
.

By Lemma 3.10 and Proposition 3.12 in
Dong and Nagatomo 1999
,
A_{\theta}(V_{L})
has the following characterization:

Lemma 7.3
.

A_{\theta}(V_{L})\cong\mathbb C[\mathbb{1}]\oplus\mathbb C[e^{\alpha}]
, with
[e^{\alpha}]=[e^{-\alpha}]
and
[e^{\alpha}]\ast_{\theta}[e^{\alpha}]=4^{-(\alpha|\alpha)}[\mathbb{1}]
.

Moreover, by Theorem 3.13 in
Dong and Nagatomo 1999
,
V_{L}
is
\theta
-rational. Thus we can apply the twisted fusion rules formula
6.7
for
V_{L}
.

Proposition 7.4
.

Let
M^{1}
be the untwisted
V_{L}
-module
V_{L}
, we have

N\textstyle\binom{V_{L}^{T_{\chi}}}{V_{L}\;V_{L}^{T_{\chi}}}=N\textstyle\binom{V_{L}^{T_{-\chi}}}{V_{L}\;V_{L}^{T_{-\chi}}}=1,\quad N\textstyle\binom{V_{L}^{T_{-\chi}}}{V_{L}\;V_{L}^{T_{\chi}}}=N\textstyle\binom{V_{L}^{T_{\chi}}}{V_{L}\;V_{L}^{T_{-\chi}}}=0.

(7.7)

Proof.

By
Theorem

6.5
and Hom-tensor duality, we have

\mathfrak{I}\textstyle\binom{V_{L}^{T_{\pm\chi}}}{V_{L}\;V_{L}^{T_{\pm\chi}}}\cong\operatorname{Hom}_{A_{\theta}(V_{L})}(T_{\pm\chi},T_{\pm\chi}).

Now
7.7
follows from
7.6
since
[E]=2[e^{\alpha}]\in A_{\theta}(V_{L})
acts on
T_{\pm\chi}=\mathbb Cv_{\pm\chi}
by
o(E)v_{\pm\chi}=E_{(0)}v_{\pm\chi}=\pm(1/2)v_{\pm\chi}
, and an element
f
in
\operatorname{Hom}_{A_{\theta}(V_{L})}(T_{\pm\chi},T_{\pm\chi})
preserves
E_{(0)}
.
∎

It remains to consider the case when
M^{1}
is the untwisted irreducible
V_{L}
-module
V_{L+\frac{1}{2}\alpha}
. We first give a spanning set of the
A_{\theta}(V_{L})
-bimodule
A_{\theta}(V_{L+\frac{1}{2}\alpha})
.

Lemma 7.5
.

A_{\theta}(V_{L+\frac{1}{2}\alpha})=\mathbb C[e^{\frac{1}{2}\alpha}]+\mathbb C[e^{-\frac{1}{2}\alpha}]
, with

[E]\ast_{\theta}[e^{\frac{1}{2}\alpha}]-[e^{\frac{1}{2}\alpha}]\ast_{\theta}[E]=[e^{-\frac{1}{2}\alpha}],\quad[E]\ast_{\theta}[e^{-\frac{1}{2}\alpha}]-[e^{-\frac{1}{2}\alpha}]\ast_{\theta}[E]=[e^{\frac{1}{2}\alpha}].

(7.8)

Proof.

Since
\alpha(-1)\mathbb{1}\in V_{L}^{-}
, we have a congruence formula similar to
7.3
:

\alpha(-m-1)v\equiv-\sum_{j\geqslant 0}\binom{1/2}{j+1}\alpha(j-m)v\pmod{O_{\theta}(V_{L+\frac{1}{2}\alpha})},

where
v\in V_{L+\frac{1}{2}\alpha}
and
m\geqslant 0
. In particular, let
m=1
, we have

\alpha(-1)e^{\frac{1}{2}\alpha}\equiv-(1/2)e^{\frac{1}{2}\alpha}\quad\mathrm{and}\quad\alpha(-1)e^{-\frac{1}{2}\alpha}\equiv(1/2)e^{-\frac{1}{2}\alpha}\pmod{O_{\theta}(V_{L+\frac{1}{2}\alpha})}.

(7.9)

Moreover, given
r\in\mathbb Z
and
u=\alpha^{1}(-n_{1})\cdots\alpha^{k}(-n_{k})e^{\frac{2r+1}{2}\alpha}\in M(1,\frac{2r+1}{2}\alpha)\subseteq V_{L+\frac{1}{2}\alpha}
, using a similar induction process as Lemma
7.1
on the degree
n_{1}+\cdots+n_{k}
of
u
, we can show that

u=\alpha^{1}(-n_{1})\cdots\alpha^{k}(-n_{k})e^{\frac{2r+1}{2}\alpha}\equiv b_{u}e^{\frac{2r+1}{2}\alpha}\pmod{O_{\theta}(V_{L+\frac{1}{2}\alpha})},

(7.10)

for some constant
b_{u}\in\mathbb C
. Now we use induction on
r\in\mathbb N
to show that

u=\alpha^{1}(-n_{1})\cdots\alpha^{k}(-n_{k})e^{\frac{2r+1}{2}\alpha}\equiv c_{u}e^{\frac{1}{2}\alpha}\ \ \mathrm{or}\ \ d_{u}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\alpha}\pmod{O_{\theta}(V_{L+\frac{1}{2}\alpha})},

(7.11)

for some constants
c_{u},d_{u}\in\mathbb C
, where
k,r\geqslant 0
,
n_{1}\geqslant\cdots\geqslant n_{k}\geqslant 1
, and
\alpha^{1},\cdots,\alpha^{k}\in\mathfrak{h}
.

When
r=0
,
7.11
follows from
7.10
. Consider the case where
r=1
. Note that
E=e^{\alpha}+e^{-\alpha}\in V_{L}^{+}
and
\operatorname{wt}E=1
. It follows from
5.5
and
6.2
that

E_{(-m-2)}v+E_{(-m-1)}v\equiv 0\pmod{O_{\theta}(V_{L+\frac{1}{2}\alpha})},\quad m\geqslant 0,v\in V_{L+\frac{1}{2}\alpha}.

(7.12)

By the definition of lattice vertex operators in
Frenkel et al. 1988
and
7.10
, we have

\displaystyle(e^{\alpha})_{(-2)}e^{\frac{1}{2}\alpha}\equiv

\displaystyle e^{\frac{3}{2}\alpha},\quad(e^{\alpha})_{(-1)}e^{\frac{1}{2}\alpha}\equiv 0\pmod{O_{\theta}(V_{L+\frac{1}{2}\alpha})},

\displaystyle(e^{-\alpha})_{(-2)}e^{\frac{1}{2}\alpha}={}

\displaystyle\operatorname{Res}_{z}E^{-}(\alpha,z)z^{-3}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\alpha}=-\frac{\alpha(-2)}{2}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\alpha}+\frac{1}{2}\alpha(-1)^{2}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\alpha}

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\lambda e^{-\frac{1}{2}\alpha}\pmod{O_{\theta}(V_{L+\frac{1}{2}\alpha})},

\displaystyle(e^{-\alpha})_{(-1)}e^{\frac{1}{2}\alpha}={}

\displaystyle\operatorname{Res}_{z}E^{-}(\alpha,z)z^{-2}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\alpha}=-\alpha(-1)e^{-\frac{1}{2}\alpha}\equiv-(1/2)e^{-\frac{1}{2}\alpha}\pmod{O_{\theta}(V_{L+\frac{1}{2}\alpha})}.

Choose
m=0
in
7.12
we have:

E_{(-2)}e^{\frac{1}{2}\alpha}+E_{(-1)}e^{\frac{1}{2}\alpha}\equiv e^{\frac{3}{2}\alpha}+\lambda e^{-\frac{1}{2}\alpha}-(1/2)e^{-\frac{1}{2}\alpha}\equiv 0\pmod{O_{\theta}(V_{L+\frac{1}{2}\alpha})}.

Hence
\alpha^{1}(-n_{1})\cdots\alpha^{k}(-n_{k})e^{\frac{3}{2}\alpha}\equiv b_{u}e^{\frac{3}{2}\alpha}\equiv b_{u}(1/2-\lambda)e^{-\frac{1}{2}\alpha}\pmod{O_{\theta}(V_{L+\frac{1}{2}\alpha})}
, in view of
7.10
. This proves
7.11
when
r=1
since
b_{u}(1/2-\lambda)
is a constant.

Now suppose
r>1
, and the conclusion holds for smaller
r
. Let
m=2r
in
7.12
, by the induction hypothesis, we have

\displaystyle(e^{\alpha})_{(-2r-2)}e^{\frac{2r+1}{2}\alpha}={}

\displaystyle\operatorname{Res}_{z}z^{-2r-2}E^{-}(-\alpha,z)e^{\frac{2r+3}{2}\alpha}z^{2r+1}=e^{\frac{2r+3}{2}\alpha}

\displaystyle(e^{-\alpha})_{(-2r-2)}e^{\frac{2r+1}{2}\alpha}={}

\displaystyle\operatorname{Res}_{z}E^{-}(\alpha,z)e^{\frac{2r-1}{2}\alpha}z^{-4r-1}\in M(1,(2r-1)\alpha/2)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle c_{r-1}e^{\pm\frac{1}{2}\alpha}\pmod{O_{\theta}(V_{L+\frac{1}{2}\alpha})},

\displaystyle(e^{\alpha})_{(-2r-1)}e^{\frac{2r+1}{2}\alpha}={}

\displaystyle\operatorname{Res}_{z}z^{-2r-1}E^{-}(-\alpha,z)e^{\frac{2r+3}{2}\alpha}z^{2r+1}=0,

\displaystyle(e^{-\alpha})_{(-2r-1)}e^{\frac{2r+1}{2}\alpha}={}

\displaystyle\operatorname{Res}_{z}E^{-}(\alpha,z)e^{\frac{2r-1}{2}\alpha}z^{-4r-2}\in M(1,(2r-1)/2)

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle c^{\prime}_{r-1}e^{\pm\frac{1}{2}\alpha}\pmod{O_{\theta}(V_{L+\frac{1}{2}\alpha})},

where
e^{\pm\frac{1}{2}\alpha}
attains the same sign in the second and fourth congruence equations.
By
7.12
,

\displaystyle e^{\frac{2r+3}{2}\alpha}

\displaystyle\equiv E_{(-2r-2)}e^{\frac{2r+1}{2}\alpha}-c_{r-1}e^{\pm\frac{1}{2}\alpha}\equiv-E_{(-2r-1)}e^{\frac{2r+1}{2}\alpha}-c_{r-1}e^{\pm\frac{1}{2}\alpha}

\displaystyle\equiv-c^{\prime}_{r-1}e^{\pm\frac{1}{2}\alpha}-c_{r-1}e^{\pm\frac{1}{2}\alpha}\equiv p_{r}e^{\pm\frac{1}{2}\alpha}\pmod{O_{\theta}(V_{L+\frac{1}{2}\alpha})}.

where
p_{r}=-c^{\prime}_{r-1}-c_{r-1}
. Now it follows from
7.10
that

\alpha^{1}(-n_{1})\cdots\alpha^{k}(-n_{k})e^{\frac{2r+3}{2}\alpha}\equiv q_{r}e^{\frac{2r+3}{2}\alpha}\equiv p_{r}q_{r}e^{\pm\frac{1}{2}\alpha}=c_{r}e^{\pm\frac{1}{2}\alpha}\pmod{O_{\theta}(V_{L+\frac{1}{2}\alpha})}.

This finishes the induction step and proves
7.11
for any
r\geqslant 0
. By adopting a similar induction argument, we can also prove
7.11
for
r\in\mathbb Z_{<0}
. Since
V_{L+\frac{1}{2}\alpha}=\bigoplus_{r\in\mathbb Z}M(1,\frac{2r+1}{2}\alpha)
, by
7.11
we have
A_{\theta}(V_{L+\frac{1}{2}\alpha})=\mathbb C[e^{\frac{1}{2}\alpha}]+\mathbb C[e^{-\frac{1}{2}\alpha}]
. Finally, by
6.1
, we have

\displaystyle[E\ast_{\theta}e^{\frac{1}{2}\alpha}-e^{\frac{1}{2}\alpha}\ast_{\theta}E]

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\operatorname{Res}_{z}\left(Y(E,z)e^{\frac{1}{2}\alpha}(1+z)^{0}\right)=[E_{(0)}e^{\frac{1}{2}\alpha}]=[(e^{\alpha})_{(0)}e^{\frac{1}{2}\alpha}+(e^{-\alpha})_{(0)}e^{\frac{1}{2}\alpha}]=[e^{-\frac{1}{2}\alpha}].

Similarly,
[E\ast_{\theta}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\alpha}-e^{-\frac{1}{2}\alpha}\ast_{\theta}E]=[e^{\frac{1}{2}\alpha}]
. This proves
7.8
.
∎

Proposition 7.6
.

Let
M^{1}
be the untwisted
V_{L}
-module
V_{L+\frac{1}{2}\alpha}
, we have

\displaystyle N\textstyle\binom{V_{L}^{T_{\chi}}}{V_{L+\frac{1}{2}\alpha}\;V_{L}^{T_{\chi}}}=N\textstyle\binom{V_{L}^{T_{-\chi}}}{V_{L+\frac{1}{2}\alpha}\;V_{L}^{T_{-\chi}}}=0,

(7.13)

\displaystyle N\textstyle\binom{V_{L}^{T_{-\chi}}}{V_{L+\frac{1}{2}\alpha}\;V_{L}^{T_{\chi}}}=N\textstyle\binom{V_{L}^{T_{\chi}}}{V_{L+\frac{1}{2}\alpha}\;V_{L}^{T_{-\chi}}}=1.

(7.14)

Proof.

We show
7.13
first. By
Lemma

7.5
we have

A_{\theta}(V_{L+\frac{1}{2}\alpha})\otimes_{A_{\theta}(V_{L})}T_{\chi}=\mathbb C([e^{\frac{1}{2}\alpha}]\otimes v_{\chi})+\mathbb C([e^{-\frac{1}{2}\alpha}]\otimes v_{\chi}).

Given
f\in\operatorname{Hom}_{A_{\theta}(V_{L})}(A_{\theta}(V_{L+\frac{1}{2}\alpha})\otimes_{A_{\theta}(V_{L})}T_{\chi},T_{\chi})
, we assume that

f([e^{\frac{1}{2}\alpha}]\otimes v_{\chi})=\lambda v_{\chi},\quad f([e^{-\frac{1}{2}\alpha}]\otimes v_{\chi})=\mu v_{\chi},\quad\lambda,\mu\in\mathbb C.

(7.15)

Recall that
o(E)v_{\chi}=E(0)v_{\chi}=(1/2)v_{\chi}
, see
7.6
. By
7.8
and
7.15
we have:

\displaystyle f([E]\ast([e^{\frac{1}{2}\alpha}]\otimes v_{\chi}))

\displaystyle=f([E\ast_{\theta}e^{\frac{1}{2}\alpha}-e^{\frac{1}{2}\alpha}\ast_{\theta}E]\otimes v_{\chi})+f([e^{\frac{1}{2}\alpha}]\otimes o(E)v_{\chi})

\displaystyle=f([e^{-\frac{1}{2}\alpha}]\otimes v_{\chi})+\frac{1}{2}f([e^{\frac{1}{2}\alpha}]\otimes v_{\chi})=(\mu+\frac{\lambda}{2})v_{\chi},

\displaystyle f([E]\ast([e^{-\frac{1}{2}\alpha}]\otimes v_{\chi}))

\displaystyle=f([E\ast_{\theta}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\alpha}-e^{-\frac{1}{2}\alpha}\ast_{\theta}E]\otimes v_{\chi})+f([e^{-\frac{1}{2}\alpha}]\otimes o(E)v_{\chi})

\displaystyle=f([e^{\frac{1}{2}\alpha}]\otimes v_{\chi})+\frac{1}{2}f([e^{-\frac{1}{2}\alpha}]\otimes v_{\chi})=(\lambda+\frac{\mu}{2})v_{\chi}.

On the other hand, we have
[E].f([e^{\frac{1}{2}\alpha}]\otimes v_{\chi})=o(E)\lambda v_{\chi}=(\lambda/2)v_{\chi}
and
[E].f([e^{-\frac{1}{2}\alpha}]\otimes v_{\chi})=o(E)\mu v_{\chi}=(\mu/2)v_{\chi}
. Since
f
is an
A_{\theta}(V_{L})
-homomorphism, we have

(\mu+(\lambda/2))v_{\chi}=(\lambda/2)v_{\chi}
and
(\lambda+(\mu/2))v_{\chi}=(\mu/2)v_{\chi}
. It follows that
\lambda=\mu=0
, and
f=0
. By
Theorem

6.5
, we have

N\textstyle\binom{V_{L}^{T_{\chi}}}{V_{L+\frac{1}{2}\alpha}\;V_{L}^{T_{\chi}}}=\dim\operatorname{Hom}_{A_{\theta}(V_{L})}(A_{\theta}(V_{L+\frac{1}{2}\alpha})\otimes_{A_{\theta}(V_{L})}T_{\chi},T_{\chi})=0.

Replacing
\chi
by
-\chi
in the argument above, it is easy to see that
\mathfrak{I}\textstyle\binom{V_{L}^{T_{-\chi}}}{V_{L+\frac{1}{2}\alpha}\;V_{L}^{T_{-\chi}}}=0.
This proves
7.13
. Next, we show
7.14
. Given
f\in\operatorname{Hom}_{A_{\theta}(V_{L})}(A_{\theta}(V_{L+\frac{1}{2}\alpha})\otimes_{A_{\theta}(V_{L})}T_{\chi},T_{-\chi}),
assume

f([e^{\frac{1}{2}\alpha}]\otimes v_{\chi})=\lambda v_{-\chi},\quad f([e^{-\frac{1}{2}\alpha}]\otimes v_{\chi})=\mu v_{-\chi},\quad\lambda,\mu\in\mathbb C.

(7.16)

With a similar argument as above, we have
(\mu+(\lambda/2))v_{-\chi}=-(\lambda/2)v_{-\chi},
and
(\lambda+(\mu/2))v_{-\chi}=-(\mu/2)v_{-\chi}
. Thus,
\mu=-\lambda
, and
\dim\operatorname{Hom}_{A_{\theta}(V_{L})}(A_{\theta}(V_{L+\frac{1}{2}\alpha})\otimes_{A_{\theta}(V_{L})}T_{\chi},T_{-\chi})\leqslant 1
. On the other hand, by Proposition 5.10 in
Abe et al. 2005
, there exists a nonzero twisted intertwining operator

\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}^{\mathrm{tw}}_{\alpha/2}(\cdot,w):V_{L+\frac{1}{2}\alpha}\longrightarrow\operatorname{Hom}(V_{L}^{T_{\chi}},V_{L}^{T_{\chi}^{(\alpha/2)}}),\quad\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}^{\mathrm{tw}}_{\alpha/2}(u,w)=\mathcal{Y}_{\alpha/2}^{\mathrm{tw}}(u,w)\otimes\eta_{(\alpha/2)+\beta},

(7.17)

where
u\in M(1,(\alpha/2)+\beta)
,
\mathcal{Y}_{\alpha/2}^{\mathrm{tw}}
is given by
7.5
,
\eta_{(\alpha/2)+\beta}:T_{\chi}\longrightarrow T_{\chi}^{(\alpha/2)}
is a linear isomorphism, and
T_{\chi}^{(\alpha/2)}=T_{-\chi}
by
7.6
and the construction in Section 5.3 in
Abe et al. 2005
. Thus we have

N\textstyle\binom{V_{L}^{T_{-\chi}}}{V_{L+\frac{1}{2}\alpha}\;V_{L}^{T_{\chi}}}=\dim\operatorname{Hom}_{A_{\theta}(V_{L})}(A_{\theta}(V_{L+\frac{1}{2}\alpha})\otimes_{A_{\theta}(V_{L})}T_{\chi},T_{-\chi})=1,

and the second equality in
7.14
can be proved by a similar method.
∎

Remark 7.7
.

We need to use the nonzero twisted intertwining operator
\tilde{\mathcal{Y}}^{\mathrm{tw}}_{\alpha/2}
in
Abe et al. 2005
for the proof of
7.14
since it is not clear from
Lemma

7.5
that
A_{\theta}(V_{L+\frac{1}{2}\alpha})=\mathbb C[e^{\frac{1}{2}\alpha}]+\mathbb C[e^{-\frac{1}{2}\alpha}]
is nonzero. Although we cannot achieve here, we believe there is an intrinsic proof of the facts that
A_{\theta}(M(1,\lambda))=\mathbb C[e^{\lambda}]
is nonzero for any
\lambda\in\mathfrak{h}
, and that
A_{\theta}(V_{L+\frac{1}{2}\alpha})=\mathbb C[e^{\frac{1}{2}\alpha}]\oplus\mathbb C[e^{-\frac{1}{2}\alpha}]
is a two-dimensional vector space.
